## M232: two-kick experiment lowers observed queue pressure, but service rate is unchanged

- Commit `e80f2f71` raised the saturated GPU progress cap from one to two per processing pass, bounded by the existing `max_count`. Release build and static Windows EXE verification passed; the visible flight manifest records this commit with a clean diff.
- The no-teleport Release flight completed naturally at `6.00002` blocks/s, focus `7→−24` (`31` chunks / `496` blocks), with `45` flight periods. It passed `24/39` general gates and `7/12` stop gates, but overall quality remained red: holes `0.9167`, dirty median/max `157/299`, fly visible-black max `23`, post-stop missing max `270`, and demand-stop convergence=false.
- Against M231's diagnostic sample, M232 had lower recorded output occupancy (pending GPU median `10` vs `11.5` of `16` slots) and active backpressure in `48/62` vs `61/62` periods. However, median kicks and finishes remained `2` each; FirstMesh schedules had raw median `3` vs `4`. Shared-world warming and different per-period admission mean this is not a causal comparison, and the run does not show a higher GPU completion rate.
- There were `3/1440` renderer ray-gap samples across two probe IDs. All hit slices had a stale published mesh (`mesh_revision` greater than `published_geom_rev`); no current-published MDI gap recurred. One sample hit `(-19,2,5)` at focus `(-16,3)`, with `mesh_revision=2`, `published_geom_rev=1`, no Dirty/work owner at the sampled pixel, and a correct `+Y` entry face (`4`). The relight audit log shows retries for this slice acquiring a mesh owner while it stayed within h4, then repeatedly hitting `dirty_admit_denied` after the route moved it to h5. Two h1 samples at `(4,2,2)` also had one-revision stale meshes and no Dirty owner at the pixel. This keeps ownerless stale geometry/admission pressure as a separate unresolved path.
- The widened forward lane remained active: `958` retained `cause=15` scheduler rows, `836` with flag bit 14, including `87` accepted h7 rows. As in M230/M231, these are admission signals, not proof of matching publication.
- Retain the two-kick cap as an experiment for now, but instrument the sampled GPU-process profile with its `max_count`, queue depth, kick/finish caps, and whether saturation progress was active. Run the same visible route again and decide from per-pass evidence whether the new cap actually fills spare readback capacity; do not infer that from the lower queue median alone. Separately trace stale geometry admission as its own obligation, especially the transition from in-range retries to denied h5 work.
- Artifacts: [M232 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m232_two_progress_kicks.json), [M232 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-015102_13824.jsonl), [M232 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-015058.13824).

## M233: the two-kick cap is active, but outer settled geometry still misses admission

- Release commit `1ca86492` built and passed the static EXE verifier. The visible no-teleport flight completed naturally at `5.99991` blocks/s, focus `7→−17` (`24` chunks / `384` blocks). This was a short route and did not reach the far checkpoint. The wrapper restored the config and users files to their recorded SHA256 values.
- The run remained well below visual acceptance: holes `0.9831`, dirty median/max `124/254`, fly visible-black max `19`, unlit max `147`, post-stop missing max `220`, focus Dirty median/end `117/117`, and demand-stop convergence=false (`19/39` general gates and `6/12` stop gates passed). A miss at focus `(-17,3)` persisted through the 102-second measured tail. These counters confirm that the queue-cap experiment did not resolve the visible debt.
- Opt-in GPU process profiling sampled `502` calls (sequence step 8). Saturation progress was active in `235` samples, each with `kick_cap=2`, queued depth at least 11, and `HasFreeReadbackSlot()==true`. Actual kicks were `0` in 31 samples, `1` in 155, and `2` in 49; finishes averaged `1.11` per sample. This proves the cap is active but is not the only limiter. The profile records only a boolean free-slot signal, so it cannot tell whether one or several of the eight readback slots were available. No causal M232/M233 throughput claim is justified from these runs.
- The relight audit log repeatedly shows ownerless geometry retries in the forward h5–h7 band ending in `dirty_admit_denied` or `light_repair_admit_denied`; successful retries nearby create a concrete mesh owner. `AdmitUnfinishedVisualDemand` currently sources its forward candidates from `PendingLightBeforeMesh`, and its two reserved positions are still subject to the separate global DirtyAdmit cap. Settled geometry debt outside the h4 unfinished cache is therefore not independently discovered, while outer candidates can be denied after selection. This is a demand discovery/admission gap, separate from GPU completion rate.
- Next, add a bounded camera-forward scan over resident slices in the existing h5–h7 approach band for current-light-settled geometry whose desired mesh revision is newer than the published revision and which has no concrete owner. Merge at most two such slices into the existing two reserved positions, and let only those reserved slices use those already-budgeted positions when the global remesh DirtyAdmit pool is empty. Keep the global mesh schedule, GPU queue, and readback ring limits unchanged. On the next visible route, verify the selected coordinates acquire a concrete owner, then follow them through GPU Ready/publication and check direct ray gaps and stop convergence.
- Artifacts: [M233 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m233_gpu_process_profile.json), [M233 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-020612_28232.jsonl), [M233 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-020608.28232), [M233 GPU process profile](../../bin/logs/gpu_process_20261001m233.jsonl).

## M234: the first outer scan excluded drawable stale meshes

- Release commit `9c80527f` passed the static EXE verifier and ran visibly, no teleport, at speed `6.00002` blocks/s. The route completed naturally at focus `7→−24` (`31` chunks / `496` blocks); config and users SHA256 values matched their preflight bytes after restoration. Manifest was clean and Release.
- The visual result remained red: holes `1.0`, Dirty median/max `108.5/204`, fly visible-black max `23`, unlit max `129`, post-stop missing max `246`, and demand-stop convergence=false. Focus Dirty fell to median/end `11/10` during stop, but the remaining missing/holes gates did not converge. GPU finishes median `1.03` versus `0.86` in M233; this route also traveled farther on the warmed shared world, so the aggregate change is not causal.
- The INFO log contained `12,510` ownerless-geometry retry rows, including `5,959` `dirty_admit_denied` and `5,787` `mesh` actions overall. In the west-facing h5–h7 band, retries still ended in admission denial; none showed a new mesh owner. The scan's `HasMeshSatisfyingColumnReady` filter caused this: that predicate deliberately returns true for any retained drawable mesh while its replacement is built, even when `desired_geom > published_geom`. The candidate census therefore skipped the exact stale-drawable case it was meant to recover.
- Remove that filter from settled geometry candidate discovery. Keep the independent revision-mismatch, current-light-settlement, no-live-owner, camera-band, and forward-sector checks; the subsequent demand path can safely retain the old drawable while admitting its replacement into one of the two reserved outer slots. Next visible run must show exact h5–h7 stale coordinates transition to `mesh`/`light_repair_mesh` with `owner_after=1`, then confirm GPU Ready/publication and stop convergence.
- Artifacts: [M234 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m234_outer_geometry_forward_admission.json), [M234 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-023519_13244.jsonl), [M234 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-023515.13244).

## M236: nearest-forward ranking does not fix h5 because the census starts at h6

- Release commit `3c7cca9a` built and passed the static Windows EXE verifier. The visible no-teleport flight completed naturally at `6.00002` blocks/s, focus `7→−24` (`31` chunks / `496` blocks), with the GUI visible. The manifest passed; config and users hashes were restored to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`. The flight runner returned 2 because the quality gate remained red.
- The run produced zero `mesh` or `light_repair_mesh owner_after=1` retries at h5; h6 had `55` mesh and `55` light-repair mesh owners, h7 had `20` mesh and `10` light-repair mesh owners. A west-sector example `(-6,3,3)` was repeatedly denied at focus `(-1,3)` while h5, then acquired a mesh owner after focus reached `(-2,3)` and the slice entered h4.
- Source tracing explains the miss: `kVisualStageFirstMeshRelightApproachHoriz` is 5, while the settled-geometry scan uses `approach+1` as its lower bound (6). Thus h5 is not eligible for either reserved outer slot; it depends on the saturated global Dirty-admit pool until it reaches h4. The M236 nearest-forward comparator changed only ordering among candidates already in the h6–h7 census and could not cover h5. Keep its near-frontier order, but extend the settled-geometry scan lower bound to h5; leave the separate pending-light scan and its light-owner rules intact.
- Whole-flight quality remains red: holes `0.95`, Dirty median/max `68/167`, fly visible-black max `28`, unlit max `139`, post-stop missing max `206`, and demand-stop convergence=false (`27/39` general, `6/12` stop gates). Against M235, these are diagnostic only: world warming, travel endpoint, and demand mix differ. M236 did reduce the Dirty median, but did not establish better visual completeness.
- Revision tracing on `(-3,3,4)` showed one sampled bump (`11→12`, `streamer_commit_sea_seam`) in the demand-transition ring. That is insufficient to explain whole-flight revision churn; broader attribution needs more source coverage.
- Next: include the h5 edge in the settled-geometry census and preserve the two-slot bound. In the next run require at least one h5 stale coordinate to acquire a concrete mesh owner before crossing into h4, then inspect publication and stop convergence. Do not treat h6/h7 owner counts alone as success.
- Artifacts: [M236 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m236_nearest_forward_frontier.json), [M236 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-030509_36012.jsonl), [M236 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-030504.36012).

## M237: h5 geometry acquires owners, while the light queue remains saturated

- Release commit `86fb560c` built and passed the static EXE verifier. The visible no-teleport route completed naturally at `5.99991` blocks/s, focus `7→−23` (`30` chunks / `480` blocks), and the manifest passed. The flight runner returned 2 because the unlit dual-lane gate remained red. Config and users hashes were restored to the same original values recorded in M236.
- The h5 extension now has direct evidence: retry rows contained `70` `mesh owner_after=1` and `55` `light_repair_mesh owner_after=1` h5 admissions. At `(-6,3,4)`, `light_repair_mesh` acquired an owner at focus `(-1,3)` while still h5; the same coordinate later recorded a stale-light publication and geometry publication through revisions `16` and `17`. h6/h7 owner counts fell to `9` and `4` respectively, consistent with the two bounded slots favoring nearer forward work. Many h5 retries still ended denied, so this proves the missing h5 path is reachable, not that the band is complete.
- Whole-flight quality did not converge: holes `0.8833`, Dirty median/max `48/208`, fly visible-black max `31`, unlit max `211`, post-stop missing max `204`, focus Dirty median/end `137/139`, demand-stop convergence=false (`18/39` general and `6/12` stop gates passed). Relative to M236 the holes and Dirty median fell while visible-black and unlit rose; the saved world is shared and the endpoint differs, so this is not a causal quality comparison.
- The next bottleneck is light admission. INFO traces show h5 work such as `(-21,2,8)` with `non_air=4096`, `field_light_rev=0`, and no settled light repeatedly returning `relight_only owner_after=0`; capture submissions for its y-band occur while it is already at h5. During the same interval the deferred-relight audit reported FIFO `16`, `40–57` in-range candidates all FIFO-blocked, and zero enqueues on most samples; the pending far/deferred set reached `491`. Thus the shared relight FIFO is saturated while visible light debt waits.
- The pending-light outer scan still starts at h6 and still rejects `HasMeshSatisfyingColumnReady`, which treats a retained drawable mesh as satisfying even when it is dark or awaits current light. In addition, `ensure_slice_relight` selects `EnqueueVisibleRelight` only for `defer_until_lit` / missing-first-mesh conditions, not every selected outer candidate; an outer stale-light repair can therefore fall back to the ordinary enqueue path. Next extend pending-light discovery to h5, remove the retained-drawable readiness filter while keeping exact current-light and live-relight-owner checks, and let only selected reserved candidates use the existing bounded visible-relight lane. Keep its FIFO and two-slot bounds; then verify a h5 light owner, settled light, mesh publication, and stop convergence.
- Artifacts: [M237 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m237_h5_geometry_admission.json), [M237 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-031702_38516.jsonl), [M237 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-031657.38516).

## M238: forward light debt falls, but material-retain retries churn geometry revisions

- Release commit `913524a5` passed the static EXE verifier. The visible no-teleport flight completed naturally at `5.99991` blocks/s, focus `7→−24` (`31` chunks / `496` blocks), with the manifest passing. Config and users hashes were restored to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- The forward-light scan reduced median pending light to `5` (M237: `13.5`), FIFO median to `15` (M237: `16`), and deferred-relight maximum to `319` (M237: `491`). h5 rows now include `18` `relight_only owner_after=1` admissions. The flight remains visually incomplete: holes `1.0`, Dirty median/max `110.5/178`, fly visible-black max `28`, unlit max `143`, post-stop missing max `270`, and demand-stop convergence=false (`18/39` general, `6/12` stop gates). These world-warming and endpoint-sensitive aggregates do not establish causality.
- The demand trace provides a direct retry-loop signature: of `1,694` geometry-revision bumps, `1,128` were `face_debt_callback`. For `(-18,3,6)`, `541` such bumps occurred over about `42.4s`; desired geometry advanced from `86` to `627` while published geometry stayed at `5`. The same coordinate recorded `257` new attempts and `257` cancellations, with lifecycle stages only at Created/Admitted. This is consistent with the publication path repeatedly rejecting a material-flipped upload, then advancing the target while a successor is being built.
- Source tracing confirms the loop edge: `GreedyGpuPublication` rejects a blockId flip when the uploaded material stamp differs from the current CPU bake, retains the old drawable, and calls `NoteFaceDebt(..., schedule_dirty=true)`. The coordinator callback unconditionally calls revision-bumping `MarkDirty`. The material check should remain strict, but when render demand already owns the current unpublished geometry target, this callback must requeue that target without advancing its revision. Only create a new target when no exact live target exists. This allows a successor publication to finish instead of invalidating it again on each retained upload.
- The analyzer's `gpu_not_ready` dominant-completion label is not itself evidence of a GPU stall here: zero-time fence polls can increment it, while `mesh_async_stuck_sec=0` and `empty_fm_completion_stuck_frames=0`. The actionable signature is the repeated face-debt target advancement. M239 should verify a sharp fall in FaceDebtCallback revision bumps and install cancellations for the same coordinates, then show published geometry catching up without weakening material validation. Also check other recurring face-debt coordinates and ensure retries still occur at the same target.
- Artifacts: [M238 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m238_forward_pending_light.json), [M238 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-033649_41160.jsonl), [M238 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-033645.41160).

## M239: material-retain revision churn drops; a direct ray finds a transient first-mesh hole

- Release commit `871e828b` built successfully and the Windows static EXE verifier passed. The visible no-teleport flight completed with manifest acceptance, speed `5.99991` blocks/s, focus `7→−23` (`30` chunks / `480` blocks); `config.json` and World_164 `users.json` were restored byte-for-byte to the same SHA256 values recorded in M238.
- The targeted churn signature fell from `1,128` `face_debt_callback` revision bumps in M238 to `40` in M239, with similarly sized traces (`12,383` versus `11,817` demand transitions). The M238 hotspot `(-18,3,6)` had no repeated face-debt bump in M239 (one attempt creation/cancellation was recorded). Another M239 coordinate, `(-23,2,5)`, had `19` face-debt bumps and `19` publication events, reaching published geometry revision `29`; this is compatible with distinct debts being retried while publication progresses. Whole-run cancellation totals are not a causal comparison because route endpoint and world state differ.
- Pixel probes provide one direct, temporary geometry-gap witness: out of `1,600` sampled pixels, one ray hit opaque block `549` at `(-385,36,63)` in chunk `(-25,2,3)` while the chunk held `4,096` non-air blocks but had no source indices or published geometry (`desired=1`, `published=0`). At that frame it had a FirstMesh/Dirty owner but no active attempt. Later probes of the same chunk showed `648` source indices and no direct ray gap. The probes found no exact-black or RGB≤8 pixels. This is sparse coverage, not proof that other visible empty regions are sound; it does show a transient missing-first-mesh path can create a real screen-facing gap and later heal.
- M239 still fails overall: holes `0.9833`, Dirty median/max `59.5/163`, fly visible-black max `28`, unlit max `171`, post-stop missing max `179`, and demand-stop convergence=false (`27/39` general, `6/12` stop gates passed). The stop snapshot's focus-miss counters reached zero, but the dual-lane light gate remained red. These aggregate differences from M238 are not causal due shared-world changes and the different endpoint.
- Next investigate forward FirstMesh ownership for loaded solid h5–h7 slices that have a Dirty/FirstMesh queue bit but no active attempt. Tie exact-hit coordinates to queue age, scheduling decisions, cancellation cause, and later source-index/publication progress; preserve the direct ray witness as the visual correctness oracle. Separately trace why visible relight completion was near zero in cruise and unlit max rose to `171`. Keep the material-stamp rejection strict and do not treat low sampled black pixels as a complete visual pass.
- Artifacts: [M239 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m239_face_debt_retry_coalesce.json), [M239 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-035915_7876.jsonl), [M239 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-035911.7876).

## M244: direct pixels separate transient FirstMesh delay from packed-face coverage gaps

- M244 (`a8450f3a`) repeated the visible no-teleport route with both relight admission and visual pixel tracing enabled. The Release manifest and route adequacy passed; speed was `5.09994` blocks/s and focus moved `7→−11` (18 chunks). Overall renderer/streaming acceptance remained red: holes `.907`, Dirty `87/237`, fly visible-black max `17`, enter unlit `35`, cruise unlit `16`, post-stop missing `70`, and demand-stop convergence=false.
- Among `2,048` pixel probes, four direct voxel-ray gaps appeared across three probes; no sampled pixel was RGB≤8. Two gaps at probe 1440 were ordinary FirstMesh delay: block `362` in chunk `(-10,2,5)` had mesh revision `1`, published geometry `0`, no source indices, and a FirstMesh Dirty owner. The same chunk later had a published mesh and no gap, so this is a real transient scheduling/publication hole.
- The other two gaps were packed-draw geometry cases and both rays entered through the +Y face. The clearest one hit block `549` at `(23,47,82)` in chunk `(1,2,5)`: mesh/published geometry were both revision `4`, the packed fallback was selected and ready, and it submitted all `15` quads in one texture-ready draw call, yet opaque depth at the voxel hit remained clear. A second packed gap hit block `549` in chunk `(4,2,2)` with stale mesh publication (`3/2`) and `22` packed quads submitted. Thus an executable draw call and current revision numbers alone do not prove that the ray-facing face was covered.
- Source comparison found a tangent-axis mismatch: GPU greedy rectangles use `u=(axis+1)%3`, `v=(axis+2)%3`, while the packed vertex shader used a different du/dv ordering for ±X and ±Y without moving the rectangle origin or swapping width/height. The +Y M244 witness belongs to that affected set. This is a source-backed root-cause candidate for the packed gaps, distinct from FirstMesh service delay and from texture lookup.
- Artifacts: [M244 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m244_visual_ray_witness.json), [M244 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-045020_25780.jsonl), [M244 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-045015.25780).

## M245: packed face tangents now match the GPU greedy grid; streaming remains unfinished

- Commit `983a6110` changed the live packed vertex shader and its embedded reference so ±X/±Y face tangents, negative-face origins, and width/height follow the axes used by GPU greedy rectangle generation. Release build and static Windows executable verifier passed (7 system DLLs).
- The visible no-teleport `product-174657` flight completed naturally with an accepted manifest, median speed `5.549955` blocks/s, focus `7→−12` (19 chunks). Config and World_164 users files were restored byte-for-byte to the same SHA256 values used in M244. Across `2,048` pixel probes there were `699` opaque voxel-ray hits and zero direct ray gaps; no sampled pixel was RGB≤8. M244 had four gaps. The exact M244 coordinate `(23,47,82)` was not hit again: the nearest matching screen-grid sample at focus `2` was at a different camera position and hit `(19,49,73)`, so this supports the face-axis fix but is not a controlled replay of the exact witness.
- The main visual/streaming failure remains: M245 holes `.767`, Dirty `85/226`, fly visible-black max `18`, enter unlit `39`, cruise unlit `6`, post-stop missing `62`, demand-stop convergence=false. The clean ray sample is encouraging for packed coverage, while persistent unfinished visual debt shows that FirstMesh scheduling, light repair, and stop convergence still need separate work.
- Next work should retain the corrected packed face mapping and use direct rays as the mesh-coverage oracle. Reproduce a packed fallback target at a stable camera/world coordinate before claiming the M244 face-gap cause is closed; then return to FirstMesh wait-to-publication latency and the bounded light-debt lane. Do not loosen visual gates or infer resolution from aggregate `holes_rate` alone.
- Artifacts: [M245 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m245_packed_axis_fix.json), [M245 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-051434_5556.jsonl).

## M246: visual missing-mesh census disagrees with the scheduling hole signal

- The Release build with snapshot-credit telemetry passed the static Windows EXE verifier (7 system DLLs). Its visible no-teleport `product-174657` flight completed naturally at median `5.09994` blocks/s, focus `7→−11` (18 chunks). Config and World_164 users files were restored byte-for-byte to SHA256 `4470F266079D264E4EBC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- Quality stayed red: holes `.9767`, Dirty `63/255`, fly visible-black max `13`, enter unlit max `36`, cruise unlit median `22`, post-stop missing `83`, and demand-stop convergence=false. The route/manifest passed adequacy; the full visual suite did not.
- New period telemetry captured `45` periods. In `17/45`, `FocusMissingMesh=0` while the independent valid focus visual census reported at least `24` MissingMesh entries (median `78` in those rows). In high-census rows, FirstMesh refreshes were frequently count-deferred, and representative scheduling was `2` FirstMesh plus `4` remesh with six available pipeline slots. The coordinator's cached hole predicate therefore does not consistently represent the loaded, undrawable backlog that the census sees. The `ColumnLoadedNoMeshN` metric is not sufficient by itself, as the prior audit established; use the census's explicit MissingMesh class.
- Snapshot refresh-budget defers sum to `1,053` (`964` FirstMesh, `89` remesh) across period rows. These are candidate attempts after refresh credits run out, not a count of missing chunks; they do not justify a blanket increase to the global refresh budget. Snapshot time was near its configured budget in most such rows, and the pipeline still limited accepted work to at most six.
- The M247 candidate feeds a valid census backlog of at least `24` MissingMesh entries into the coordinator's existing `missing_visible_mesh` predicate. This should activate the existing hole/FirstMesh admission policies where the old sampled signal was zero. Snapshot time and pipeline caps remain unchanged. Retain only if the next visible run improves FirstMesh service and direct visual coverage without regressing queue age or stop convergence.
- Artifacts: [M246 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m246_snapshot_budget_telemetry.json), [M246 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-054720_12636.jsonl).

## M247: census-driven admission changes service mix, but not visual convergence

- The first census-driven coordinator candidate built in Release and passed the static verifier. In a visible no-teleport flight at median `5.1` blocks/s, FirstMesh scheduling median rose `2→4` while remesh scheduling fell `1→0`; cruise unlit median changed `22→11`, and post-stop missing changed `83→73`. Whole-run holes stayed `.9767`, fly visible-black max rose `13→19`, Dirty median changed `63→119`, and demand-stop convergence still failed. These are directional shared-world measurements, not a cold-cache causal A/B; retain no quality claim from them.
- Code review found the candidate had incorrectly gated `FocusVisualMissingMeshN` on `FocusDataCensusValid`. That flag enables only the expensive voxel-occupancy diagnostics when `CUBA_VISUAL_BLACK_TRACE` is set; the ordinary focus render-readiness classification is available without it. Thus M247 exercised the new policy only because its diagnostic flight enabled the trace flag. The flag gate is removed in the follow-up, and M248 will verify that the ordinary telemetry signal reaches the policy with diagnostics off.
- M247 supports that the old sampled miss signal can suppress FirstMesh service while the focus census reports missing drawable meshes, but service redirection alone has not repaired holes and can starve remesh work. Keep the global schedule and snapshot time limits. Continue with separate FirstMesh-to-publish and LightRepair service evidence before broadening the admission changes further.
- Artifacts: [M247 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m247_visual_backlog_signal.json), [M247 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-060054_41872.jsonl), [M247 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-060050.41872).

## M248: normal render-readiness telemetry activates the backlog policy; short-route quality remains red

- M248 ran visibly on Release with `CUBA_VISUAL_BLACK_TRACE` unset. The ordinary `FocusVisualMissingMeshN` remained populated (median `24`, maximum `81`) while `FocusDataCensusValid` was `0`; FirstMesh schedule median remained `4`. This verifies the corrected gate uses production render-readiness telemetry rather than the opt-in voxel diagnostic.
- The route completed naturally at `5.1` blocks/s and focus `7→−11` (18 chunks). Whole-run holes remained `.9767`, visible-black max `25`, Dirty median/max `129/205`, cruise unlit median `8`, post-stop missing `85`, and demand-stop convergence=false. The A24 near-hole safety line passed, but dual-lane stop-line and full visual acceptance failed. The one-flight comparison to M247 is not causal because the route reuses a changing world; it does confirm that the policy is active without trace instrumentation and still needs end-to-end validation.
- Because this 18-chunk product route does not reach the far-distance checkpoints, it cannot resolve the original long-flight regression. Keep the telemetry-driven policy only as an evaluated candidate; next run the existing visible `product-174657-far` no-teleport route with logs, then inspect exact ray-hit chunk lifecycle and GPU-ready publication at distance. Do not treat the shorter corridor's A24 pass as a streaming fix.

## M249: far flight finds transient FirstMesh gaps and an ownerless partial-column debt class

- The Release `product-174657-far` flight ran visibly without teleport and completed naturally at median `5.99853` blocks/s. Focus moved `7→−96` (about `103` chunks / `1,648` blocks), but the configured far checkpoint was not reached. `config.json` and World_164 `users.json` were restored byte-for-byte to their established SHA256 values.
- Overall visual acceptance remains red: holes `.9451`, visible-black maximum `26`, Dirty median/maximum `167/306`, cruise unlit median `23.5`, and demand-stop convergence=false. The direct-pixel sample had `516` solid hits, `1,266` hits stopped by unloaded chunks, and `266` rays without a solid within the horizon; there were three direct ray-gap events and no sampled RGB≤8 pixels. Sparse pixels do not establish that visible empty regions are correct.
- The three ray gaps were at focus `−95`, in solid chunks `(-99,2,1)` and `(-99,2,2)`: each had 4,096 non-air blocks, mesh revision `1`, published geometry `0`, source index count `0`, and a queued FirstMesh attempt. Both later built and published geometry revision `6` in about `56ms` and `51ms`. This confirms a transient FirstMesh scheduling/publication hole at distance; it does not implicate terrain generation or the packed fallback in these particular samples.
- Of `508` sampled focus-slice traces classified `focus_state=1`, decoding the named owner fields shows `311` had an actual mesh, relight, or ColumnFlow owner, while `197` had none. All `197` ownerless rows carried only overlay face debt (`peer_face_debt_mask=0`), with desired coverage `1` and published coverage `0`; several coordinates recurred across many trace frames. The old diagnostic state therefore overstates ownerless debt by about 61%, but a substantial repeatable ownerless subset remains.
- A source-level admission gap is now under investigation. `ClassifyFocusColumnVisual` returned `TerrainIncomplete` before consulting `GetColumnRenderableState`; this can suppress the progressive FirstMesh demand for a resident solid slice while the rest of its terrain column is still being generated. M249 traces report `ColumnEmergeState::Empty` on the ownerless samples, consistent with that path but not a direct per-coordinate terrain-completion proof. Next, admit incomplete columns only when the renderer reports a resident `MissingMesh`, and make the diagnostic census count queued/inflight relight and ColumnFlow owners separately from mesh owners; validate both with a Release flight before claiming a streaming improvement.
- Artifacts: [M249 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m249_far_visual_backlog.json), [M249 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-061359_36876.jsonl), [M249 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-061355.36876).

## M251: missing-mesh candidates are admitted, but their concrete owner does not persist

- The Release `product-174657-far` flight completed naturally at `5.99853` blocks/s, moving focus west `98` chunks (`1,568` blocks proxy distance); its manifest passed and the far checkpoint was not reached. The process exited normally. Config and World_164 users files were restored byte-for-byte to SHA256 `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- Visual acceptance remains red: holes `1.0`, Dirty median/maximum `127/245`, fly visible-black maximum `26`, cruise unlit median `23`, effective FPS about `19.15`, and stop convergence=false. This is diagnostic evidence from a reused world, not a controlled quality comparison.
- The new focus-worklist fields separate the 1,024 focus-slice rows into `295` state-1 `MissingMesh` rows whose columns are in `unfinished_keys`, `213` state-1 `Ready` rows outside that worklist, and `516` state-2 rows with another owner. Thus M251 did not reproduce the old worklist omission for the persistent `MissingMesh` class. Keep the `Ready`/outside-worklist rows separate; they are not evidence that the same admission path should service them.
- Seven recurring solid slices in the `MissingMesh`/worklist class had stable chunk incarnation and content revision `4096`, mesh revision exactly one ahead of published geometry, desired coverage `1` versus published `0`, overlay debt only, and no mesh/relight/ColumnFlow owner in every one of `42–43` final focus snapshots. Their demand attempts were repeatedly reminted. At the same coordinates, the INFO log recorded `action=mesh owner_after=1` retries about every two seconds. Therefore the admission callback sees an owner immediately after marking, but that owner is absent from later per-frame ownership snapshots; the present traces do not identify which operation removes or transfers it.
- The last recorded `mesh_schedule_trace` for these seven coordinates predates their final ownerless snapshots by roughly `430` frame epochs. Earlier scheduler records are dominated by `RefreshCountBudget` deferrals (`mesh_snapshot_defer_reason=1`) with some accepted submissions (`cause=15`). These records show FirstMesh capture-budget pressure, but cannot yet prove it causes the later owner loss. Chunk incarnations stayed constant, so stream unload/reload does not explain the sampled disappearance.
- Next instrument the admission-to-scheduler handoff and every queue-owner terminal transition for the exact `MissingMesh` coordinates: record Dirty lane/index and queue age immediately after admission; then record capture defer/accept, queue removal, inflight/GPU ownership, and cancellation/cleanup with the same coord, incarnation, revision, and attempt ID. Specifically distinguish a submission that completes without publish from a queue erase or a Dirty mark rejected/deduplicated by another live owner. Keep global caps unchanged until this boundary is identified. Do not use the `Ready` rows to broaden demand.
- Artifacts: [M251 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m251_focus_worklist_trace.json), [M251 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-072754_35744.jsonl), [M251 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-072750.35744).

## M252: actual first-mesh demand is throttled by the output backpressure cap

- The visible no-teleport far flight completed normally at `5.99853` blocks/s, west focus `7→−96` (`103` chunks / about `1,648` blocks). Its acceptance report remained red. The direct voxel-ray probe recorded `22` gap events across `11` solid chunks; these are concrete missing-geometry samples, not only the aggregate `holes` counter.
- At focus `−81`, periods with unresolved visible mesh had a median `202` FirstMesh Dirty entries and `7.5` free output slots, while the late output backpressure fence requested `16` schedules and allowed only `4`. Across `108` periods with both visual debt and backpressure, that cap stayed at `4`; the dual-lane split then scheduled a median `3` FirstMesh jobs and `1` remesh. In nearby direct-ray gaps, FirstMesh rows waited at queue positions `11` and `176` in a queue of `218` while the renderer had no geometry for the hit voxel.
- This identifies a bounded-pipeline throughput mismatch: the queue had unused output headroom, but the fixed `kBackpressuredScheduleCap=4` ignored it during a non-normal backlog. The normal-admission cap remains useful; under a measured visual backlog the safe bound is the lower of admission capacity and currently free output slots. M253 applies that narrow change and measures both output occupancy and FirstMesh service.
- Focus rows at `cy=0/1` are a separate census class: each sampled chunk held `4,096` non-air blocks, published no indices, had overlay debt only, and had no direct ray-gap witness. With camera `y=56`, these low chunks and their full non-air peer boundaries are not evidence for the visible y=2 ray gaps; keep them out of the visual root-cause count unless an on-screen probe reaches them.
- Artifacts: [M252 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m252_owner_after_retry.json), [M252 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-081812_33252.jsonl), [M252 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-081808.33252).

## M253: using output headroom raises FirstMesh service, but moving-front gaps remain

- Commit `97c082c7` removes the unconditional four-job clamp from the backpressured non-normal path. Scheduling now uses `min(output_headroom, admission_schedule_cap)`; normal admission still uses its former cap of `4`. The Release build passed the static Windows executable verifier (6 system DLLs). No tests were run.
- The visible no-teleport `product-174657-far` flight completed naturally at `5.99853` blocks/s and moved west `104` chunks (about `1,664` blocks). The app exited normally and config/users hashes were restored to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`. The flight harness returned exit code `1` because visual stop-lines failed; route adequacy and process completion passed, and the far checkpoint was still not reached.
- The policy change is visible in telemetry. Across `86` visual-debt/backpressure periods, available schedule capacity had median/max `5/15` (M252: `4/4`), actual total schedules had median/max `5/12` (M252: `4/4`), and FirstMesh schedules had median/max `4/11` (M252: `3/4`). Median output headroom was `5`, so work remains bounded by real slots rather than an arbitrary global increase. Direct ray gaps fell from `22` events in M252 to `11` in M253 (7 distinct hit chunks), but the reused world and different samples make this a directional signal rather than a controlled causal A/B.
- Overall acceptance is still red: holes `.9877`, Dirty median/max `146/240`, visible-black max `19`, cruise unlit median `3.5`, cruise schedule-ok median `5`, and effective fly FPS about `22.46`. The gap witnesses remain filled y=2 chunks at the moving frontier. Some had no active attempt or Dirty owner at the exact probe; several others were queued or in a Created attempt. Thus increasing service capacity reduces the transient set but does not close the demand-to-first-publication latency.
- A second, distinct churn signal appears at `(-68,2,5)`: the attempt target advanced from geometry revision `2` to `7` through `priority_dirty_enqueued`, `priority_world_core_commit`, and `priority_relit_install_repair` bumps, with repeated cancellations before publication at revision `7` roughly `2.6s` after the first attempt. This is a legitimate source-update sequence until proven otherwise; do not suppress revision validation. Next trace whether a build had captured each source revision when those bumps arrived, and separate pre-capture target coalescing from in-flight supersede. Preserve a provisional drawable when possible, then let a single successor publish the latest revision.
- Artifacts: [M253 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m253_output_slot_headroom.json), [M253 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-110025_25132.jsonl), [M253 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-110021.25132).

## M254: revision churn happens before capture, while a Dirty owner already exists

- The Release build with bump-time owner tracing passed the static Windows EXE verifier (6 system DLLs). The visible no-teleport far flight completed normally at `5.99853` blocks/s, west focus `7→−91` (`98` chunks / `1,568` blocks). Config/users hashes were restored to the established values. The harness returned `1` because overall visual gates failed; route adequacy and process completion passed, and the far checkpoint remained unreached.
- The opt-in demand ring recorded `1,552` mesh revision bumps. At every one, active source revision, pending capture, capture-ready, async build, GPU extract, pending GPU apply, queued GPU and kicked/dispatched GPU were all false. At the post-bump sample, `1,311` entries were in ordinary RemeshQ and `241` in FirstMeshQ. `1,438` bumps belonged to an active render-demand attempt. This shows the observed cancellations do not protect a source snapshot already being built or uploaded: the queue has not captured that revision yet.
- Six direct ray-gap events hit four solid y=2 chunks near focus `−68`. For `(-71,2,1)`, six bumps and eight cancellations occurred; the final bump raised geometry revision `5→6` while a Dirty queue item existed and no capture/GPU owner was present. The other three hit coordinates showed the same queued-before-capture pattern. This is more precise than merely seeing an owner disappear after retry.
- Overall acceptance remains red: holes `.9571`, Dirty median/max `167/230`, visible-black max `26`, cruise unlit median `4`, cruise schedule-ok median `4`, effective fly FPS about `19.26`. The eye-proxy stop-line passed. As with earlier flights, reused world state and sparse screen probes prevent attributing aggregate differences to one change.
- M255 should coalesce only a revision bump whose exact demand attempt is still active, whose matching Dirty entry is queued, and for which no capture/source/GPU owner exists. In that case invalidate the capture-store entry and let the queued job bake the newest block/light state against the existing target revision. Keep revision advancement and supersede behavior whenever any source snapshot or GPU stage is already owned; retain the material/current-revision publication checks. Validate that cancellation/remint counts and ray gaps fall without stale publication.
- Artifacts: [M254 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m254_revision_owner_trace.json), [M254 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-112640_23196.jsonl), [M254 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-112637.23196).

## M255–M256: pre-capture revision coalescing prevents scheduler recovery

- M255 (`12537d4b`) retained a queued demand attempt when a mesh revision bump arrived before capture, while invalidating the capture-store snapshot. The visible Release flight reached focus `−99` at nominal speed, but the result regressed sharply: Dirty median/max `1,242/2,961`, render-ready columns median `111`, `293` install publications, and `712` direct pixel-ray gap samples. Its demand trace recorded `1,220` coalesced bumps; `1,148` already had a nonzero published geometry revision, and `1,050` had `published_geom_rev == mesh_revision_before`. Thus the first implementation also risked leaving current published geometry stamped as current.
- M256 (`671d585c`) added a guard for a cached mesh at the current revision. This reduced the trace to `696` coalesced events, but the same route remained red: Dirty median/max `894.5/2,415`, render-ready median `171.5`, only `220` install publications, and `716` direct ray gaps. The repeated low publication count and high unfinished geometry show that preserving an `Admitted` demand without its source owner does not reliably restart scheduling. The central M255 hypothesis—queued attempt churn is merely redundant—was wrong; revision advancement is part of the existing recovery path.
- Both experimental commits were reverted as `16dfb334` and `672b36ef`. The restored Release M257 run returned Dirty median/max to `168/297`, recorded `1,414` install publications, and regained M254-scale unfinished-visual rate. This is a strong regression signal from M255/M256, though the no-teleport flights are not pixel-identical A/B runs. Keep strict revision advancement until a scheduler handoff fix can prove otherwise.
- Artifacts: [M255 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m255_precapture_coalesce.json), [M256 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m256_published_revision_guard.json), [M257 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m257_after_coalesce_revert.json).

## M257: direct pixel gaps are resident geometry waiting behind the lit gate

- The reverted Release route completed naturally at `5.99853` blocks/s and reached focus `−94` (`101` chunks / about `1,616` blocks); the manifest was clean. Config and `World_164/users.json` hashes matched their saved baselines after the run. Product acceptance still failed; this is a diagnostic baseline, not far-flight acceptance.
- The sparse exact pixel-ray probe found `16` direct gaps across `10` hit chunks. Every hit had non-air voxel data and no published source/index or GPU draw payload. Thirteen samples were `PendingLight` plus `defer_until_lit`, with no settled field revision; their relight queues were either priority positions or the bounded deferred-visible lane. Three already had settled light and FirstMesh Dirty membership. At focus `(-84,3)`, for example, `(-87,2,3)` had `4,096` non-air blocks, active demand at `Admitted`, no drawable payload, pending light, and a priority relight position `6/21`. This confirms the user-visible “empty chunk” can contain solid terrain while its mesh is intentionally gated on lighting.
- Capture telemetry during cruise had median cap `1` background column (p95 `2`), median async relight in-flight `1`, median FIFO `15`, and median focus PendingLight `2` (p95 `21`). The broader schedule blocker was `empty_fm_queue`; however the direct hit traces show two separate states: settled slices waiting in Dirty scheduling, and unlit slices waiting behind Relight-before-mesh. This makes blanket FirstMesh admission or raising the global mesh cap an incomplete fix.
- The existing provisional-light preview already gives the renderer an ambient fallback for a newly built first mesh, but SoftDefer only opens that path for one nearest missing column or selected hinterland cases. The direct misses included multiple forward columns at horizontal distance three, where the normal LitDrawable ring still defers their first mesh. Next evaluate a small, explicitly bounded near-forward preview set using the existing provisional flag and renderer path; retain the strict gate for remeshes and preserve current lit geometry. Acceptance requires direct ray gaps to fall without black/blue preview artifacts, source revisions to remain current, and post-stop convergence. If the scheduler stall is fixed instead, the exact settled hit must transition from Dirty queue to source capture/owner and publish before leaving the view.
- Artifacts: [M257 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-122442_32664.jsonl), [M257 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-122438.32664).

## M258: bounded lateral preview did not provide a safe streaming fix

- M258 (`3cedba93`) allowed the two same-height lateral neighbors of the current exact missing-mesh witness to publish first-mesh ambient previews, retaining the existing lit gate for remeshes. The Release build and static EXE verifier passed. The visible GUI no-teleport `product-174657-far` run completed naturally at `5.99853` blocks/s, with focus `7→−75` (`82` chunks / `1,312` blocks). It did not reach the far checkpoint; the harness returned `2` because the dual-lane `unlit_max` gate failed. Config and World_164 users hashes were restored to their established values.
- The sparse direct pixel-ray trace saw `20` gap samples in `17` unique chunks. Thirteen samples had PendingLight with `defer_until_lit=0`, four still had PendingLight plus `defer_until_lit=1`, and three had no PendingLight (one of those had some source indices but still missed the ray). M257 had `16` samples in `10` chunks, thirteen with PendingLight plus `defer_until_lit=1`. The run routes stop at different foci and reuse a changing persistent world, so neither the hole-rate change (`.9571→.9379`) nor the per-sample class shift is a causal A/B result.
- The clearest safety signal is accumulated provisional-light work: `focus_provisional_light_preview` rose from M257 median/max `44/73` to `311/412`; unlit debt max rose `53→196`, and the scheduler's dominant blocker was `zero_fm_cap`. M258 also had effective fly FPS `11.89`, cruise PendingLight median `5`, and post-stop not-ready median `244`. The bounded three-column rule was local per witness, but as the witness moved it accumulated a broad population of provisional chunks without clearing the direct gaps or the stop convergence gates.
- Reverted the preview experiment in `aa288d72`. Do not reopen lateral first-mesh preview as a global policy. Future progress should target exact visible-miss scheduling/lighting ownership and FirstMesh capacity, while keeping provisional preview single-witness and bounded in time as well as space.
- Artifacts: [M258 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m258_bounded_forward_preview.json), [M258 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-130506_16796.jsonl), [M258 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-130503.16796).

## M259: the pinned nearest-miss witness is not the screen-facing gap

- Joined the exact `renderer_pixel_voxel_ray_gap` probe rows to period telemetry at the same focus coordinate. In M257, all `16/16` direct gap samples had a different XZ coordinate from the period's active `MissCx/MissCz`; at focus `(-84,3)`, for example, the ray hit chunks `(-87,2,3..6)` while the streaming witness stayed at `(-85,3,3)` (`MissHoriz=1`). In M258, all `19/19` gaps with an active witness also differed; at focus `(-58,3)`, ray hits included `(-63,2,7)`, `(-62,2,9)`, and `(-61,2,9)`, while the witness was `(-63,2,-1)` (`MissHoriz=5`). The twentieth M258 gap had no active witness at its matching focus sample.
- The mismatch is consistent with `FindNearestMissingGreedyMesh`: it returns a resident solid candidate from the first non-empty horizontal Chebyshev ring and uses vertical distance for tie-breaking. It does not project candidates into the current camera frustum or follow a screen ray. Downstream miss pinning, relight promotion, and the single-witness provisional exception can therefore work correctly on a different chunk while the visible pixel gap stays open. This explains why improving a generic nearest target did not guarantee the renderer-facing holes improved.
- Do not widen the provisional exception around that ring-selected witness. The next implementation should produce a small screen-facing candidate set from the camera view and resident voxel data, then route only a bounded number of confirmed missing-drawable targets through existing FirstMesh/Relight ownership. Keep `renderer_pixel_probe` as verification; do not make synchronous framebuffer readback a production scheduler dependency. Acceptance must correlate the exact direct hit with its owner transition and GPU publication, not only with aggregate holes.
- This finding is diagnostic and does not claim M258 caused the mismatch: M257 already shows it on the reverted baseline. M258's policy was reverted in `aa288d72`; M258 evidence was committed in `285d43d7`.

## M260: first screen-ray selector missed the renderer probe scanlines

- Commit `906cf8cf` moved the opaque voxel DDA used by renderer probes into the shared world raycast API and added a bounded CPU screen-ray candidate selector plus `miss_screen_ray_candidate` telemetry. Release build and static Windows EXE verification passed (7 system DLLs). The first visible no-teleport run had no renderer pixel trace; it is retained only as route/performance evidence.
- The trace-enabled visible run used the same Release binary and clean commit, completed naturally at median `5.99853` blocks/s, and moved west `99` chunks (`1,584` blocks proxy distance). The app exited normally; harness product gates failed (`unlit_max=50`, and A24 saw 22 periods with near-focus holes). It did not reach the far-distance checkpoint. Config and users-file SHA256 values were restored to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- The trace contains `2,048` renderer pixel samples and four direct ray gaps, all from probe `4800`. Each hit the same resident solid slice `(-61,2,4)` with `4,063` non-air blocks, no drawable/satisfying render flags, and PendingLight at priority relight queue index `5`; no Dirty mesh owner was present. At the matched period/focus, the ring-selected miss witness already pointed to `(-61,2,4)`, so these four gaps do not demonstrate a ring-witness mismatch in M260.
- `miss_screen_ray_candidate` stayed `0` across all 165 period rows. The first selector sampled five rows at normalized top-left Y `.25/.50/.75`; renderer probes instead found gaps on GL-bottom-left scanlines `y=90` and `270` (the apparent 1280×720 grid maps these to top-left `.875` and `.625`). The sparse sampler therefore did not test the screen bands where the gaps appeared. This is a selector-coverage defect, not evidence that the shared ray DDA or the missing-slice filter rejected the hit.
- The follow-up samples five fixed X columns (`.1/.3/.5/.7/.9`) across the renderer's four scanline bands (`.125/.375/.625/.875` in top-left coordinates). It still selects at most one resident solid missing slice and does not read the framebuffer. Verify candidate-source activity against the same pixel rows, then follow the selected coordinate through relight/FirstMesh ownership and GPU publication before calling this a fix.

## M263: empty pixels span both unpublished and stale drawable geometry

- The Release no-teleport run completed normally at median `5.99853` blocks/s and covered focus `7→−64` (`71` chunks / `1,136` blocks proxy distance). The route remained short of the far-distance checkpoint. The harness rejected product gates (`unlit_max=59`, dual-lane failure); A24 passed. Post-stop demand convergence failed. Config and World_164 users hashes returned to their established values.
- The trace had `2,048` renderer pixel samples and two direct opaque-ray gaps. At frame `2640`, pixel `(480,270)` hit resident slice `(-36,2,5)`: `4,096` non-air blocks, no published geometry, and a Dirty owner at queue index `67/219`, age `14` frames. The same-focus period row's selected witness was `(-36,2,7)`, but period data aggregates 27 frames and cannot be joined to that pixel as a same-frame selection.
- At frame `1800`, pixel `(160,270)` hit resident slice `(-18,3,6)` with `910` non-air blocks and depth `1.0` (no opaque fragment at the sampled pixel). Unlike the first gap, this slice reported drawable/satisfying/draw-ready state, CPU opaque+transparent refs, and `2,070` source indices; its visible MDI command had `600` indices. At the same time its desired geometry revision was `8`, while the published geometry revision was `7`; face-debt mask was `63`, a FirstMesh attempt was active, and the pixel ray had no PendingLight. Thus one user-visible symptom covers both a missing first publication and an older retained image that does not cover a current opaque voxel.
- This disproves a single-cause model in which every empty screen region waits for first mesh streaming. A drawable cache entry can still be stale against demanded geometry/coverage. The screen selector must count revision debt on drawable slices and route it through bounded repair ownership; changing the global retain-old-image policy would risk restoring previously fixed whole-chunk voids.

## M264: same-frame join confirms the selector filtered out a stale drawable gap

- The Release visible flight completed normally at median `5.99853` blocks/s, with `flight_move_speed_scale=1`, a clean manifest, and focus `7→−65` (`72` chunks / `1,152` blocks proxy distance). This did not reach the far-distance checkpoint. The harness failed dual-lane quality (`unlit_max=56`), A24 (`2` near-focus-hole periods), and post-stop convergence. The application exited normally; config and users-file SHA256 values were restored.
- The new selector-event ring filled its `4,096`-record capacity, retaining frames `4292–4766` and evicting earlier route evidence. Four direct pixel gaps were present, but only the frame-`4320` gap still had its selector sample in the dump. Limit opt-in selector traces to the renderer's `60`-epoch probe cadence so a complete route fits the ring, and sample framebuffer pixel centers (half-pixel offsets) exactly.
- At frame `4320`, renderer pixel `(1056,90)` had pre-transparent depth `1.0`; the independent voxel DDA hit opaque block `(-935,39,31)` in chunk `(-59,2,1)`. The selector sampled the corresponding `20×4` screen tile at top-left `(1056,630)` and hit block `(-935,39,30)` in that same chunk (the half-pixel correction was not yet present). It was in focus radius and height band, but `HasMeshSatisfyingColumnReady` returned true, so `screen_ray_candidate=0` and the chunk was not selected.
- The same-frame pixel slice had mesh revision `6`, published geometry revision `3`, desired geometry revision `6`, no PendingLight, no Dirty queue entry, no mesh work-owner flag, and an active demand attempt whose last progress was about `1.17s` old. The cache's immediate “drawable means satisfying” shortcut therefore hid a concrete geometry-publication debt from the selector while the old image left an opaque screen ray uncovered.
- The next repair keeps old drawable data available to the renderer, but classifies matching-incarnation geometry/coverage debt as refresh work for the bounded screen selector. Its FirstMesh owner path must be allowed to requeue that existing revision only after confirming no GPU or mesh worker owns it. Exact renderer pixel-center rays and same-epoch trace retention will verify both selection and publication.

## M265: pixel-center joins work, but one ranked witness misses multiple visible debts

- The Release visible run completed normally at median `5.99853` blocks/s and covered focus `7→−62` (`69` chunks / `1,104` blocks proxy distance). It did not reach the far-distance checkpoint. The harness failed dual-lane (`unlit_max=48`), eye-proxy blink, A24 (`37` periods with near-focus holes), and post-stop convergence. The clean manifest used `flight_move_speed_scale=1`; config and users-file hashes were restored.
- Restricting opt-in selector traces to 60-epoch probe frames retained the full sampled route without ring overflow: `820` ray records over `41` epochs, including all `2,048` renderer pixel samples. Six direct pixel-ray gaps were recorded. The corrected pixel-center transform produced exact same-block and same-chunk CPU-ray joins at frames `1740` and `1800`.
- At frame `1740`, pixel `(96,270)` hit opaque block `(-283,49,97)` in `(-18,3,6)`, with empty renderer depth. The screen selector hit the exact same block at tile column `1`, row `2`; the slice was drawable/satisfying but had geometry debt (`desired_geom=9`, `published_geom=7`) and a stale active attempt. It was admitted as a candidate but not selected. The one selected coordinate was `(-17,2,4)`, seen by two rays.
- At frame `1800`, pixel `(352,270)` and the exact screen ray both hit `(-314,46,87)` in `(-20,2,5)`, also with empty renderer depth and geometry debt. That candidate was admitted but not selected; the center-ranked primary was `(-22,2,3)`, which had no geometry debt. This shows the current single-witness ranking can choose a nearby missing slice while ignoring a stale drawable gap on another ray in the same view.
- The next bounded change should admit a small ranked set of distinct screen targets per probe, retaining stale geometry debt ahead of ordinary missing-mesh candidates and passing each selected debt through ColumnFlow's existing owner checks. Continue to score same-frame visible evidence without using framebuffer readback in production. M265 does not validate a user-visible fix: all product stop-lines remain red and direct gaps persist.
