## M266: bounded selector can see a first-mesh gap but does not admit it

- The Release visible no-teleport run completed at median `5.99853` blocks/s and covered focus `7→−62` (`69` chunks / `1,104` blocks proxy distance). The route did not reach the far checkpoint. The manifest was clean and config/users hashes returned to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`. Product gates remain red: holes `.9664`, frontier-pressure fraction `.880`, near-void maximum `1,844`, visible-black maximum `31`, Dirty median/maximum `209/323`, focus PendingLight median `7`, and fly wall-time median `56.0 ms`. Composite post-stop convergence remains false.
- Across `2,048` pixel probes there were nine direct opaque-ray gaps in six chunks. Five gaps were first-mesh queue entries in four chunks (`(-64,2,4)` at `82/270`, `(-53,2,6)` at `63/254`, `(-39,2,1)` at `0/221`, and `(-38,2,4)` at `80/207`); their queue ages were `5`, `17`, `34`, and `11` frames. Three pixels hit the same stale drawable chunk `(-18,3,6)` with desired geometry `8` versus published `1`, but no live Dirty owner and no same-frame screen-ray selector witness.
- The remaining first-mesh gap, `(-21,2,6)` at frame `2040`, had no mesh revision, demand target, or Dirty owner at the pixel sample. The exact same-frame CPU screen ray did find it (`mesh_satisfying=0`, `geometry_debt=0`, `candidate=1`), but it was not among the top three selected chunks. The previous selector only sent selected geometry-debt coordinates through ColumnFlow, so ordinary first-mesh candidates outside the primary near-focus column could remain unqueued. M267 reserves one bounded screen-ray repair slot for a hit without a satisfying mesh, keeps the other slots for geometry-debt repair, and deduplicates selected slices by horizontal column because ColumnFlow has one FirstMesh ticket per column.
- At frame `2760`, screen-ray selection did match a direct gap in `(-38,2,4)`, but that slice already had a FirstMesh owner at queue position `80/207`. Later demand traces show its geometry target advancing from `1` to `15` through four distinct bump sources (world-core repair, chunk-emerge repair, relit-install repair, and sea-seam commit); publication reached `9` before repeated superseded attempts left it at `15/9`. Keep this revision churn separate from selector coverage: do not weaken source-stamp validation without proving which producer advances stale inputs.
- Artifacts: [M266 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m266_top3_screen_repair.json), [M266 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-155456_38876.jsonl).

## M267: first-mesh ray candidates are admitted, while a gap remains behind the light gate

- Commit `e8cf55bb` reserves one of four bounded screen-ray repairs for a hit without a satisfying mesh, keeps up to three remaining slots for geometry debt, and avoids multiple vertical slices competing for one ColumnFlow ticket. The Release build and static EXE verifier passed; no tests were run.
- The visible run completed normally at median `5.99853` blocks/s and covered focus `7→−67` (`74` chunks / `1,184` blocks proxy distance). It remained product-red: holes `.9667`, visible-black maximum `25`, Dirty median/maximum `146.5/204`, focus PendingLight median `6`, fly wall-time median `43.4 ms`, frontier-pressure fraction `.945`, and stop convergence=false. The number of direct pixel gaps varied from M266's nine to one in this reused-world run, so this is not a controlled visual improvement claim. The run manifest was clean; the config and users hashes match their established values.
- The selector traced `960` ray rows over `48` eligible epochs and selected at least one first-mesh candidate in `14` epochs, confirming the reserved category is exercised. Its one direct pixel gap at frame `4800`, pixel `(544,270)`, was in `(-64,2,4)`: `4,096` non-air blocks, mesh revision/publication `0/0`, desired geometry revision `1`, active demand attempt `48210`, and no Dirty mesh owner. The direct pixel and CPU selector rays were not same-chunk joins at that epoch: the selector phase sampled X columns `1,5,9,13,17`, while pixel X `544` maps to column `8`.
- That chunk was `PendingLight=1`, `async_relight_inflight=1`, `defer_until_lit=1`, with no relight FIFO entry; its ColumnFlow ticket was `RelightThenMesh`. The mesh gate is intentionally waiting for light, so admitting a mesh build around it could publish unlit geometry. Across the route, median relight completions stayed `0`, deferred-far relight debt reached `311`, FIFO dropped `707` entries, and false clears reached `7`. Next instrument the exact async relight lifecycle and gate ownership (capture, worker start/done, completion drain, apply/finalize, retry/drop) for the pixel-hit chunk; do not remove the light gate based on these data.
- Artifact: [M267 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m267_visible_first_mesh_repair.json), [M267 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-161458_19804.jsonl).

## M268: relight completes; sampled holes have mesh-publication owners

- The visible Release no-teleport run completed normally, with focus moving `7→−67` (`74` chunks / `1,184` blocks proxy distance); config and users hashes matched the established baseline. The application exited successfully, but product gates remain red: holes `.925`, visible-black maximum `22`, Dirty median/maximum `221.5/302`, focus PendingLight median `6`, fly wall-time median `46.6 ms`, frontier-pressure fraction `.890`, and near-void maximum `1,841`. Post-stop demand convergence is false. The dual-lane line failed only on `unlit_max=49`; the A24 near-focus-hole line also failed, while the eye-proxy line passed.
- Four of `2,048` direct pixel probes hit opaque terrain without matching renderer depth. Three at frame `4080` were in `(-48,2,3)` and `(-48,2,4)`: desired/published geometry was `1/0`, and both chunks had live FirstMesh queue owners (positions `35/216`, age `13` frames, and `49/216`, age `3`). The fourth at frame `3240`, `(-36,2,5)`, had desired/published geometry `1/0` while a mesh worker owned it; `PendingLight=1` at that sample. These observations point to work still moving through mesh publication, not evidence for bypassing the light gate or a lost owner.
- The relight lifecycle audit observed `5,007` job snapshots/start/done records and `4,671` apply records; `336` jobs retried after their captured dependency input changed. Compute time was about `47 ms` median, `102 ms` p95, and `191 ms` maximum. Of `1,701` ownerless solid applies, first-mesh repair was requested for all; an owner was confirmed afterward for `1,610`. No worker-stuck signature appeared. Separately, `(-36,2,5)` accumulated geometry debt from desired revision `1` to `11` while published geometry stayed at `6`; retries generally acquired queue ownership, then the final retry was denied after focus had moved away. Later extraction of this run's bump trace identified ten increments: `priority_world_core_repair`, `priority_world_streaming_commit`, `priority_relit_install_repair`, three `priority_dirty_enqueued`, `priority_chunk_emerge_repair`, and three `streamer_commit_sea_seam`. The latter seam bumps arrived after earlier owners had drained, so each legitimately advanced the rebuild target; batching or relaxing source validation needs evidence that the seam inputs are stable.
- The output-pipeline fence had median output headroom `7` slots and median admitted schedule cap `6` across `122` perf periods; it was active in `88`. In active periods the FirstMesh/Remesh caps had medians `5/1`, while actual scheduled counts had medians `4/1`. Some period-end telemetry showed an empty Remesh queue and zero remesh schedules while the earlier lane-cap sample still reserved one Remesh slot. Queue counters are refreshed at different points in the frame, so this is not yet proof that the fence wastes capacity. If this becomes the next optimization target, record fence-time queue counts alongside its lane decision and actual scheduled counts before changing the split or transfer policy.
- This run is not a controlled A/B against M267: the world was reused, the direct-pixel gap count is small, and the broad holes gate remains high. It confirms that the new first-mesh selector path is only one part of the problem; end-to-end draw publication, queue age, and geometry revision churn remain relevant. Keep the light gate intact pending a case where a lit, current, drawable-absent slice has no advancing owner.
- Artifacts: [M268 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m268_relight_audit.json), [M268 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-162555_32120.jsonl), [M268 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-162551.32120).

## M269: selector phases are covered; sampled gaps are in owned work

- Commit `6c0cab65` increased the opt-in screen-ray trace cadence to every 15 streaming frames and doubled its bounded ring. The Release build completed and the static EXE verifier passed. The visible no-teleport flight exited normally at median `5.99853` blocks/s, moved focus `7→−52` (`59` chunks / `944` blocks proxy distance), and did not reach the far checkpoint. Product gates remained red: holes `.9661`, visible-black maximum `13`, Dirty median/maximum `181.5/292`, focus PendingLight median `7.5`, fly wall-time median `70.9 ms`, frontier-pressure fraction `.832`, and near-void maximum `5,190`. It passed `24/39` gates; the dual-lane line failed at `unlit_max=51`, the eye-proxy blink line and A24 near-focus-hole line failed, and post-stop demand convergence remained false. This reused-world run is not a controlled A/B against M268.
- Trace volume increased to `2,840` screen-ray rows over `142` epochs. All 20 horizontal columns appeared, with the four phases represented `34–37` times apiece; this allows same-route-position joins that M268's lower-cadence trace could not provide. Nine direct pixel gaps appeared in five chunks. The primary cluster was four pixels at frame `2880` in `(-42,2,5)`: non-air `4,096`, desired/published geometry `1/0`, current slice light settlement `1/1`, but `PendingLight=1`; the mesh Dirty queue was FirstMesh position `3/228`, age `38` frames, and the relight queue position was `11/24`. An exact-phase screen ray selected that same first-mesh chunk. Subsequent relight audit records show it became drawable within several seconds; no orphaned owner was observed.
- The other sampled gaps also had concrete progress owners: `(-20,2,6)` had multiple mesh/GPU owners and a same-frame selected geometry-debt ray; `(-17,3,6)` had current field light and a Remesh queue owner; `(-7,2,2)` had a `RelightThenMesh` ticket and FIFO owner despite no Dirty mesh owner at the pixel sample; `(-7,2,3)` had both relight and FirstMesh queue ownership. The sampled gaps therefore do not point to a selector that failed to notice visible targets or to a safe reason to remove the light gate. They expose queue-to-publication latency and large competing FirstMesh backlog.
- The `(-42,2,5)` revision trace recorded `1→2` from `priority_relit_install_repair`, then `2→3→4` from two `streamer_commit_sea_seam` bumps. The mesh published geometry revision `2` before those later seam updates; the newer revisions represent fresh neighbor inputs and should not be accepted as already satisfied without a replacement build. Continue investigating how to reduce the first-mesh queue age and streaming/seam backlog while retaining source-stamp correctness.
- M269 artifacts: [report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m269_selector_phase_trace.json), [perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-171217_9336.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-171213.9336).

## M270: exact-slice lighting gate fixed; visible-flight backlog remains

- Commit `763deaeb` changes targeted FirstMesh admission to test the requested Y-slice's light settlement rather than blocking it on any PendingLight debt in the whole column. A screen-ray hit with unresolved slice light now promotes an existing queued relight while preserving the FIFO pin, or submits a bounded `RelightThenMesh` ticket when no queue/in-flight owner exists. The `MeshLitGate` remains in force. The opt-in ray trace now records `screen_ray_light_debt` for the exact hit slice. Release build passed the static EXE verifier (7 system DLLs); no tests were run.
- The visible no-teleport Release flight completed normally at median `5.8493` blocks/s, focus `7→−62` (`69` chunks). It met route adequacy but did not reach the far checkpoint. Config/users hashes were restored to `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C` and `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- Product acceptance stayed red at `24/39`: holes `.9328`, visible-black maximum `19`, Dirty median/maximum `152/212`, focus PendingLight median `8`, fly wall median `55.8 ms`, near-void maximum `1,842`, stop missing maximum `83`, and stop convergence=false. The main scheduling blocker remained `empty_fm_queue`; cruise relight completion median was `0`, deferred-far relight debt reached `204`, FIFO drop delta was `621`, and false-clear delta was `10`. Async mesh-stuck time remained zero. The broad hole metric still reports unfinished visualization and cannot establish whether every visually empty chunk has empty voxel structure.
- The new field recorded `3,860` screen-ray rows over `193` epochs, with all 20 horizontal columns observed. There were `1,254` ray-confirmed repair candidates and `831` selected rows; `113` selected rows (`95` unique slices) had unresolved exact-slice light. This confirms the visible selector exercised the new light-debt branch. It does not by itself prove FIFO promotion succeeded or that the owner later published; some matching lifecycle snapshots still ended at `started`/`admitted` with geometry behind desired revision.
- Relative to M269, focus traveled farther (`69` vs `59` chunks), holes and Dirty counts were lower, but visible-black peak was higher, relight completion median fell (`1`→`0`), FIFO drops and false clears rose (`329/3`→`621/10`), and product gates remained `24/39`. Because the reused world, corridor extent, and remaining queue state differ, this is directional evidence only, not a causal A/B. The target-slice fix closes one demonstrated queue gate bug but does not solve streaming throughput or publication latency.
- Next, trace the selected screen-ray request through its actual action result: exact FIFO key found, priority promotion accepted/refused (including active-pin protection), async owner observed, fallback Flow ticket accepted/deduped, and subsequent capture/apply/publish outcome. Join these events to the same coordinate, incarnation, desired/published revisions, and queue age. In parallel investigate why visible FirstMesh debt coexists with an `empty_fm_queue` scheduling blocker and why FIFO drop/false-clear counts rose; do not increase global caps or weaken light/source validation based on this reused-world run.
- M270 artifacts: [report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m270_exact_slice_light_repair.json), [perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-175109_19812.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-175104.19812).

## M271: visible relight requests reach FIFO/worker, mesh tickets often dedupe or reject

- Commit `64b288ec` adds opt-in `ScreenRayRepair` handoff logs at the actual screen-ray action point: exact slice light debt, async ownership, relight queue kind/index/size before and after, and FirstMesh/RelightThenMesh Flow ticket membership before and after. This stays in `WorldStreaming.cpp`, so the Release rebuild compiled only that translation unit and passed the static EXE verifier (7 system DLLs).
- The visible no-teleport Release flight completed normally at median `5.70007` blocks/s, focus `7→−61` (`68` chunks). It passed route adequacy and did not reach the far checkpoint. Product acceptance remained red at `25/39`: holes `.9496`, visible-black maximum `20`, Dirty median/maximum `186/257`, PendingLight median `8`, fly wall median `53.3 ms`, near-void maximum `1,824`, stop missing maximum `71`, stop convergence=false. The dual-lane/eye-proxy/A24 lines failed. Config/users hashes remained at the established values.
- The opt-in audit logged `690` selected repair actions. For light debt: `55` queued entries were moved to priority index `0` (they were already in the priority deque at indices up to `23`), `57` RelightThenMesh tickets were present afterward (`9` newly added, `48` already present), one relight ticket was rejected, one queued column was also async-owned, and four hits had async ownership without a FIFO key. Among these action events, later exact-column audit lines showed a worker snapshot/completion for `40/55` FIFO promotions and `28/57` Flow-ticket-present events; later applies were observed for `34` and `24` events respectively. Events can repeat a column, so these are not unique-slice success rates.
- FirstMesh ticket observations were `115` present (`45` newly added, `70` pre-existing) and `457` rejected/deduped. Several high-frequency rejected coordinates later published the desired geometry revision (for example `(-9,2,3)` at `13/13`, `(-17,2,4)` at `5/5`, and `(-8,2,3)` at `3/3`), confirming that at least some rejections correspond to real in-flight completion. The current handoff log does not yet expose ColumnRecord stage/token or exact mesh/GPU owners at rejection time, so it cannot classify all rejected requests as safe deduplication versus stale record ownership.
- Despite successful queue promotion and many worker applies, cruise relight completion median remained `0`, deferred-far debt peaked at `244`, FIFO drop delta was `616`, and the overall holes gate stayed high. The dominant schedule blocker changed from M270's `empty_fm_queue` to no dominant blocker; completion stalls were classified as `gpu_not_ready`. This suggests the selected handoff is executing but does not close end-to-end voxel-to-publication latency at the moving front.
- Next, enrich the action log with the live ColumnRecord stage/pending token, any ColumnFlow ticket, mesh build/extract in-flight, pending GPU apply, drawable/satisfying mesh, and exact geometry debt at the same coordinate and epoch. For rejected FirstMesh tickets, this will show whether an active owner justifies dedupe or a record claims Meshing/GpuPending without an executable owner. Keep global scheduling caps and mesh/light validity gates unchanged until that distinction is measured.

## M275: route ownerless slice debt into the Dirty service

When a selected screen-ray FirstMesh request is rejected and its exact slice has no Dirty/build/GPU owner, the code marks only that lit slice into the existing mesh Dirty service. The fallback is bounded by the selected ray set and preserves exact-slice light settlement. Release build and static EXE verifier passed.

Trace recorded 240 direct marks across 171 unique slices. Of 79 unique targets later sampled again, none was observed debt-free; only five showed a mesh-owner transition in those sparse samples. This proves Dirty admission, not end-to-end repair.

## M279: relight revision churn fell, while screen-confirmed geometry gaps remained

- Commit `dbcfcb3c` prevents `PriorityRelitInstallRepair` from advancing geometry revision when an unpublished newer geometry target already exists; the relight repair still requeues that target and validates its captured inputs. Release build and static EXE verification passed; no tests were run.
- M279 used the same `180 s` phase and route speed scale as M277, but the world was reused, the focus path covered fewer chunks (`7→−55`, 62 chunks), and the trace ring is capped. In the captured demand-transition window, `priority_relit_install_repair` bumps fell from `64/1,729` in M277 to `3/1,819` in M279. The overall captured bump count did not fall: `face_debt_callback` rose `16→227`, `priority_world_core_repair` `199→271`, and `priority_dirty_enqueued` stayed high (`488→466`). This verifies the targeted suppression path, not an overall throughput or visual improvement; do not coalesce these other sources without proving their dependency inputs are unchanged.
- Three of 2,048 renderer pixel probes found an opaque CPU voxel-ray hit without a matching rendered surface. At `(-22,2,4)`, geometry was `1/0` (desired/published) with a Dirty owner at queue index `75`, age `4`. At `(-17,2,6)`, geometry was `2/1` with no Dirty/build/GPU owner at the sample, despite an active demand target. At `(-14,2,2)`, the slice had pending light, geometry `1/0`, and sat at Dirty queue index `114`, age `11`. These are direct observations of present world data whose visible geometry was missing or stale; the sparse probes do not establish the total screen-hole rate.
- Product gates remained red (`25/39`): unfinished-visual holes `1.0`, visible-black maximum `23`, Dirty median/maximum `178.5/255`, focus PendingLight median `6`, fly wall median `54.24 ms`, near-void maximum `1,843`, and demand-stop convergence=false. Post-stop missing reached `81`; the empty-world and enter-dirty-residual lines passed. The run did reproduce the empty-looking chunks without evidence that their block data was absent.
- The captured transition window recorded `1,819` geometry revision bumps. In the target event snapshots no mesh source/capture/worker/GPU owner was active at the instant of a bump; this is consistent with bumps being enqueued before a successor owner is acquired and does not by itself identify churn. Screen-ray handoff logs recorded `284` rejected FirstMesh requests, `112` already-present tickets, and `52` direct slice marks. The rejected requests often coincide with an active column job, so they are not evidence of a lost owner by themselves.
- The visual/demand rings do not provide a reliable one-to-one history for the three pixel hits: pixel-probe samples and transition records cover different portions of the reused-world route, and incarnation matching eliminated the apparent same-coordinate history. Next trace the exact hit through one shared bounded timeline (frame epoch + incarnation + desired/published geometry + queue owner), then follow that attempt through capture, source-stamp validation, apply, and publish. Keep the validity gates and global schedule caps unchanged while this handoff is unjoined.
- Config and `World_164/users.json` hashes matched their established baselines after the flight. Artifacts: [M279 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/relight_lifecycle_product174657_20261001m279_relight_revision_trace.json), [perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-211912_34556.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-211908.34556).

## M280: VisualObligation shadow matches the existing draw gate; streaming remains the bottleneck

- The Release visible no-teleport flight kept `CUBA_VISUAL_OBLIGATION_CUTOVER` off and enabled only the non-mutating shadow classifier. The run moved at `5.99853` blocks/s and covered focus `7→−49` (`56` chunks); it did not reach the far checkpoint. The exact config/users hashes returned to their baseline values.
- The shadow classifier sampled `890,404` draw decisions and disagreed with the current gate `10` times (`0.0011%`). This does not support switching the draw predicate as the next hole fix. No renderer policy changed during this run.
- Product gates remained red (`25/39`): unfinished-visual holes `.9038`, visible-black maximum `20`, Dirty median/maximum `194/266`, focus PendingLight median `6`, fly wall median `57.89 ms`, near-void maximum `1,839`, and post-stop convergence=false. The report's streaming-phase median was `41.49 ms` (`stream_ms=23.49`, mesh emerge `17.44`); raw period-row medians were `40.34/23.28 ms`. `chunk_not_ready` median was `69` and frontier-pressure fraction was `1.0`. The observed work backlog is upstream of the draw-ready decision.
- The M279 lifecycle trace also has a same-incarnation churn example at `(-14,2,2)`, incarnation `1621`: over `8.3 s`, geometry revision advanced `1→16` while published geometry reached `15`. The captured 15 bump events were nine `priority_dirty_enqueued`, five `priority_chunk_emerge_repair`, and one `streamer_commit_sea_seam`. In the first `2.9 s`, the target advanced `1→13` while publication reached `10`; some intermediate attempts were canceled and others published before the next target. This coordinate incarnation is later than M279's pixel-probe incarnation, so it is not a causal join to that pixel miss. Treat it as evidence that seam/emerge work can create repeated valid successors on a moving frontier; do not suppress those updates without checking their source stamps.
- The shadow run is not an A/B for M279: it used a reused world, reached fewer chunks, and shadow instrumentation adds per-slice CPU work. It nevertheless rejects the hypothesis that broad mismatch between the two readiness policies explains the current missing geometry. Next profile the work that fills the first-mesh/relight lanes and the measured streaming phase; leave draw cutover and source validation unchanged.

## M281: exact-ray first-mesh requests reached drawable state; remesh debt still falls to the ordinary queue

- Commit `a6cf9704` adds one bounded direct FirstMesh mark for the exact selected screen-ray slice when it has light debt and no drawable or active mesh/GPU owner. The existing relight ticket and all snapshot/source/light validation remain in place. The Release visible no-teleport run used speed scale `1`, moved at `5.99853` blocks/s, and covered focus `7→−57` (`64` chunks / `1,024` blocks proxy distance). It passed `26/39` product gates; config and `World_164/users.json` hashes matched their established baselines. The run is not a controlled A/B and the far checkpoint was not reached.
- The new `relight_plus_unlit_preview_dirty` action occurred twice. At `(-17,2,4)`, the exact ray mark was followed about `0.75 s` later by a drawable mesh with published geometry `1`, published light `0`, and current field light `1`; this is consistent with the existing stale-light ambient-preview path. At `(-42,2,6)`, the first mark found no drawable and a FirstMesh queue owner at `0/121`; the handoff snapshot about `0.42 s` later still showed no drawable. The logs do not prove the user-visible pixel outcome for either target, and this reused-world flight cannot establish causality.
- The second target exposes a follow-on scheduling gap: after it acquired a drawable predecessor, its desired geometry revision continued from `4` to `10` while published geometry remained `4`. Repeated `unowned geometry debt retry` records left it in ordinary Remesh queue kind `3` (for example, index `49/50`), rather than the priority Remesh lane. `EnsureVisualRepairDirtyPriority` requeues an already-unpublished revision without a bump, but unlike the direct `MarkMissingSlicesDirtyPriority` path it does not then promote the drawable geometry-debt remesh. This is a bounded, evidence-backed queue-ordering fix to pursue; it must preserve the existing total scheduling budget and validity checks.
- Product symptoms remained broad: holes `.9333`, effective holes `.9333`, visible-black maximum `17`, Dirty median/maximum `123/192`, focus PendingLight median `8`, fly wall median `45.16 ms`, frontier-pressure fraction `.989`, `chunk_not_ready` median `60`, and post-stop convergence=false. The eye-proxy stop line passed; A24 near-focus holes, the dual-lane unlit maximum, and post-stop convergence failed. Empty-world and enter-dirty-residual lines passed. These passing lines do not show that visually empty chunks contain no voxel data.
- Telemetry distinction found while joining the preview: `chunk_meshed_unlit_preview` is assigned from `FocusDarkMeshPreview` (sticky-remesh preview), while `focus_provisional_light_preview` counts drawable slices that qualify for the separate provisional-light path. The former stayed zero in all 202 period rows; that does not say whether a provisional-light preview was drawn. The latter was nonzero but has no per-coordinate join. Add a bounded coordinate-level mark→capture→publish→draw trace before using either aggregate as the outcome for an exact ray witness.

## M285: GPU profiling rules out long fence waits as the broad blocker

- M285 ran the visible no-teleport Release scenario at speed scale `1` and measured movement `5.99853` blocks/s. It reached focus `7→−98` (`105` chunks / about `1,680` proxy blocks), but the harness did not classify this as the far-flight checkpoint. Product acceptance remained red at `22/39`: holes `1.0`, visible-black maximum `27`, Dirty median/maximum `770/1,535`, frontier-pressure fraction `.935`, FirstMesh Dirty median `113`, scheduled-work median `5`, relight-completion median `0`, FIFO drops `1,032`, and post-stop missing maximum `87` with convergence=false. The reused world was more heavily loaded by this longer run, so comparison with M284 is directional only.
- The opt-in GPU process profiler emitted `2,550` samples (one for every eighth `ProcessPendingGpuMeshes` call). Per sampled call, pending output was `9→8` at the median, one result finished, and total CPU time was `1.264 ms` median / `3.491 ms` p95. Counter-fence wait was `.0077 ms` median / `.0174 ms` p95; quad-finish `NotReady` occurred in only `50` operations across the captured sample. Counter `NotReady` was observed, but usually as non-blocking polling; the profile does not show a long driver fence or quad-readback stall.
- The accompanying raw period rows had median pending-GPU count `10`, median output occupancy `8/16`, active output backpressure in `73/89` fly periods, and a median FirstMesh/Remesh schedule result of `4/1`. The GPU pipeline keeps making progress while pressure limits new schedule admissions. The broader CPU phase medians were about `19.5 ms` mesh-emerge, `5.0 ms` mesh snapshot, and `5.4 ms` scheduling; profiler revision validation was `.343 ms` median / `.887 ms` p95 per sampled call. These facts support investigating main-thread scan/admission cost and the finite output queue together. They do not justify expanding the queue or readback ring yet, since M285 also had 225 long spikes and a roughly `50.5 ms` median fly period.
- Exact file hashes for `bin/config.json` and `bin/worlds/World_164/users.json` matched the established baselines. Artifacts: [M285 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/gpu_finish_profile_product174657_20261001m285.json), [perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-225757_33896.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-225752_33896), [GPU profile](../../bin/bin/logs/gpu_process_profile_m285.jsonl).
- Next, preserve a cold/canonical baseline and profile CPU scans/admission with a per-stage work count, then choose a bounded budget change from measured queue age and near-focus FirstMesh latency. Keep the GPU output cap, source validation, and route speed unchanged until a paired run shows that a new budget improves near-camera publication without worsening frame spikes or seam validity.

## M286: packed neighbor-fluid reads reduce capture cost; visible holes remain

- The M286 patch based on `80b44957` reads each loaded face neighbor's packed fluid byte directly from its already-resolved chunk during `ChunkMeshSnapshot::Capture`. This removes repeated chunk-map hash lookups and unpack/repack work for up to `6 × 16 × 16` shell cells per capture. The access is equivalent to `UChunkManager::GetFluidState`: both use `LocalIndex(WorldToLocal(worldPos))` for a loaded chunk, and both yield empty fluid for an unloaded neighbor. Fluid mutation advances the same chunk content revision already included in capture validity.
- The Release `Cubatarium` target built successfully and `verify-windows-exe.ps1` passed with seven system DLL dependencies. The all-target Release build returned code `1` on unrelated auxiliary test-target errors: missing `glm/glm.hpp` in several test targets and an unresolved `CubatariumLogInfo` in `decor_mesh_integration_test`. No tests were run.
- The visible no-teleport `product-174657-far` run used the same route hash as M285, a `300 s` fly phase, scale `1`, and measured `5.99853` blocks/s. Focus moved `7→−99` (`106` chunks; M285 `7→−98`, `105` chunks). The M286 dirty diff hash was `e590d618667cd19c342de54697c85ef48bf64c35d069c38b0422fc855243d8b5`; config and `World_164/users.json` hashes matched their established baselines.
- Relative to the M285 report, snapshot median fell from `5.0262 ms` to `4.0803 ms` (`−18.8%`), scheduled-mesh phase median from `5.4216 ms` to `4.00695 ms` (`−26.1%`), and mesh-emerge median from `19.51025 ms` to `17.99425 ms` (`−7.8%`). Dirty median improved `770→733.5`, maximum `1,535→1,449`, `chunk_not_ready` median `73→66.5`, and FIFO-drop delta `1,032→647`. This is directional evidence from two cold-labeled runs, not a controlled A/B.
- Product quality remains red and did not improve: holes stayed `1.0`; gate count fell `22/39→14/39`; fly wall median rose `50.5153→53.59885 ms`; focus PendingLight median rose `6→8`; post-stop missing rose `87→88`; convergence remained false; relight completion median remained `0`. The optimization improves a measured CPU stage but does not explain or repair the missing geometry. The app-level Release build and route verify the patch is safe to retain as a local performance improvement, not as a solution to the rendering failure.
- Artifacts: [M286 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m286_shell_fluid_lookup_product174657_20261001.json), [perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-233720_37720.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-233716.37720).
- Next, instrument candidate discovery/admission with per-period counts and queue-age/source-stage joins, since `empty_fm_queue` still dominates scheduling while `cruise_relight_completed_med=0`. Keep global caps, source validation, and priority ordering intact until a paired flight shows an increase in near-camera drawable publication and stop convergence without additional frame spikes.

## M287: voxel census confirms solid resident terrain is missing meshes

- A visible 120 s no-teleport diagnostic flight enabled `CUBA_VISUAL_BLACK_TRACE` so the bounded focus census could inspect occupancy and mesh ownership. Tracing reduced route throughput: measured speed was `5.70007` blocks/s and focus covered `7→−36` (`43` chunks), so this is diagnostic evidence, not a performance comparison. The run manifest was clean at commit `47d491ac`, scale `1`, same route hash; protected config/users hashes matched their baselines.
- The analyzer omits the census fields from its summary JSON, but the raw perf log contains `72/74` valid point-in-time census rows. Median focus census: `1,126,828` non-air voxels across `360` resident solid slices. In the visual vertical band, median counts were `171.5` drawable meshes, `182` solid slices without a drawable, `125.5` of those with pending work, `35` unresolved with no mesh/relight/flow owner, and `43` accepted-empty solid slices. Median GPU draw-gate-closed count was `0`; `chunk_not_ready` exactly matched `draw_oracle_missing_resident` (`67.5`), while false-negative culls were `0` at the median. This confirms real voxel data is present while a large portion of its slice geometry is absent; it does not prove that every accepted-empty slice should emit faces.
- The bounded focus-slice trace captured solid slice `(−39,1,6)` at horizontal distance `3` from focus `(−36,3)`: all `4,096` blocks are non-air, its `−X` boundary has `256` non-air voxels, that peer chunk is absent, and `−X` is in both face-debt and overlay-debt masks. The slice had no drawable mesh, dirty entry, in-flight mesh, GPU work, or active attempt; the column was `MissingMesh` and remained in the unfinished set. Its desired, mesh, and published geometry revisions were all `115`. Since this route travels toward `−X`, it is an exposed candidate in the flight direction, though this trace is not a pixel hit.
- Current policy maps a true unloaded peer to `NeighborLoadState::Unknown`, and the mesher hides that boundary face. History shows this conservative rule was retained to prevent distant closing walls; `74eea2f0` only makes absent `+Y` faces provisional. The new evidence makes near-view missing side faces a plausible contributor to empty-looking chunks and identifies unresolved per-slice ownership as a second issue. Do not globally reinterpret Unknown as Air. Next prototype a tightly bounded provisional side-face mask for missing peers in the near-forward ring, preserve the missing-neighbor overlay and face debt, and let the normal peer-arrival seam remesh replace the provisional surface. Validate both no-hole coverage and distant-wall count on the same route, and ensure accepted-empty internal slices remain valid.
- Artifacts: [M287 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m287_focus_occupancy_product174657_20261001.json), [perf and focus-slice traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-234942_40808.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261001-234938.40808).

## M288: screen misses are stale mesh publications, not a proven side-face policy defect

- Joined the 2,048 M287 renderer-pixel probes to the voxel occupancy and mesh-publication fields. Of 663 rays that hit a loaded opaque voxel, three samples had no opaque depth surface. All three target slices had current geometry newer than published geometry: two samples hit `(-18,3,6)` at `9/8` (current/published) with a visible mesh and visible MDI commands; one hit `(-7,2,5)` at `1/0` in the FirstMesh queue. The other 660 voxel-hit samples had a depth surface. This is a small diagnostic sample, not a total-screen error rate.
- The two misses in `(-18,3,6)` were at world blocks `(-285,49,96)` and `(-283,49,97)`. The first lies on the `z=0` chunk boundary; the second is inside the slice at local `(5,1,1)`. The target had a priority Remesh owner at index `98/342`, age `110` frames, geometry target `9`, publication `8`, and two visible MDI commands (`600` visible indices). One other probe hit the same slice and did have depth. Thus the data confirms a localized stale-geometry gap and does not establish that Unknown-neighbor culling caused it; a side-face fallback would not address the interior hit.
- Demand transitions for that incarnation show the geometry-9 attempt repeatedly canceled and replaced while publication remained at 8; a later attempt published geometry 9 about `13.5 s` after the first captured cancellation. The trace does not include enough per-frame mesh-owner state to prove why the priority remesh waited. The separate FirstMesh miss in `(-7,2,5)` was queue index `53/99`, age `10`; its geometry-1 install published within milliseconds of the recorded transition, so that isolated sample does not justify raising FirstMesh admission globally.
- Across the flight's period rows, median focus-band occupancy was `360` solid slices and `182` without drawable meshes (`125.5` with pending work; `35` unresolved without an owner). Median `chunk_not_ready` again matched missing GPU-resident geometry (`67.5`); median false-negative culling was zero. Median mesh schedules were `5` FirstMesh plus `1` Remesh per period. The output pipeline held a median `8.5/16` jobs, with backpressure active in `54/74` periods and median post-backpressure schedule capacity `6`. The Release run was trace-heavy and is diagnostic only.
- This refines M287: real solid voxel data is present, but geometry can remain stale or unpublished while the screen ray already hits it. Do not prototype provisional side faces based on this evidence. Next trace the same coordinate through dirty-queue selection, snapshot refresh budget, GPU extraction/readback, and publication; determine whether output headroom or revision/attempt churn is limiting completion before changing queue capacity or admission caps.
- Artifacts: [M287 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m287_focus_occupancy_product174657_20261001.json), [perf and transition traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261001-234942_40808.jsonl).

## M289: watched traces expose aged near-focus Remesh work

- Commit `bb694883` adds a bounded schedule trace for chunks observed by the renderer's voxel-pixel probes. Each attempted schedule now records queue position and age, admission result, geometry target/publication, and active work owners. The Release `Cubatarium` target built and its static executable verifier passed. No tests were run.
- The visible no-teleport `product-174657-far` route used scale `1`, 80 s movement, and 10 s stop; it measured `5.70007` blocks/s and reached focus `7→−21` (`28` chunks). The new watched ring retained `512` events: `399` accepted schedules, `69` refresh-budget deferrals, `33` emerge-budget deferrals, `9` enqueue rejections, and `2` schedule-time-budget deferrals.
- Watched slice `(-18,3,6)` was accepted at frame epoch `2415` with priority Remesh at queue index `0/398`, age `532` schedule frames, mesh/current geometry `11`, and publication `10`. This confirms a drawable slice can carry an old geometry target for hundreds of scheduling frames even when it finally reaches the queue head. The trace begins at the selected attempt; it does not identify every intervening frame that kept the item out of service.
- Pixel probe `(-338,42,80)` hit solid data in `(-22,2,5)` with no depth at epoch `2040`, FirstMesh index `82/188`, age `12`, revisions `1/0`. The watched schedule trace records that slice admitted at epoch `2046`, then its successors through geometry `12` admitted from the queue head. Later samples caught revision `13/12` (target/publication) still at priority Remesh index `116–120/398`, ages `134–254`; those later attempts were not selected during the retained watch window. A second depth miss in `(-20,2,6)` sat at priority Remesh `103/368`, age `44`. These joins show queue wait in addition to the transient FirstMesh handoff.
- Route-level quality remained red: holes `.7955`, Dirty median/max `347/593`, fly wall median `41.27 ms`, post-stop convergence=false, and `15/39` product gates. Focus-band medians remained `360` solid slices, `183` without drawable mesh, `115` with pending work, and `33.5` unresolved without an owner. Median `chunk_not_ready` again equaled missing GPU-resident geometry (`73.5`). This instrumentation improved attribution, not rendering quality.
- This evidence does not support a provisional unknown-side-face change. The next bounded behavior change is queue fairness for *aged priority Remesh entries inside the focus radius*: keep current total schedule caps and FirstMesh slots, but promote already-prioritized Remesh debt after a measured age threshold so recurring nearer insertions cannot leave it at index `100+`. Re-run the same visible route and compare near-focus queue age, drawable census, wall spikes, and stop convergence before retaining the change.
- Artifacts: [M289 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m289_watch_schedule_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and watched traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-004152_41152.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-004148.41152).

## M290: priority Remesh prefix invariant was incomplete

- Commit `7f282095` added age ordering for the presumed contiguous priority Remesh prefix. The Release target and static verifier passed. A visible no-teleport run at scale `1` traveled 28 chunks, focus `7→−21`, with `5.70` blocks/s. The app exited normally; the harness marked product gates red.
- Route metrics were `17/39` gates, holes `.9773`, Dirty median/max `313.5/471`, fly wall median `43.60 ms`, `chunk_not_ready` median `70`, and post-stop convergence=false. Compared with M289, missing geometry remained high (`73.5→70`), while the no-drawable census (`183→189`), pending-work census (`115→126`), and unresolved-without-owner census (`33.5→45`) did not improve. These two sequential runs share the route and speed but are not a controlled A/B because World_164 and cold-start state can evolve.
- The watched trace retained `512` schedule attempts. Of `194` priority Remesh samples, the median selected index was `0`, but some were as deep as index `108`. More specifically, `(-22,3,6)` at focus distance `3` and age `126` still appeared at index `15`; `(-22,2,6)` at distance `3`, age `136`, appeared at index `9`. Both are older than the configured 30-frame threshold, so the new ordering did not reach all priority entries.
- Cause: `PrioritizeAgedPriorityRemeshNearHorizontal` only searched the initial priority prefix, but `PrioritizeChunksWithoutMesh` stable-sorted RemeshQ by drawable absence without re-partitioning priority entries. Focus invalidation can therefore leave ordinary Remesh work before old priority repairs; the fairness method silently misses the latter. M290 does not validate the intended treatment. Restore the prefix invariant after every queue reorder, then rerun before judging the hypothesis.
- Artifact: [M290 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m290_aged_remesh_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and watched traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-010022_16820.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-010018.16820).

## M291: visible ray gaps split into first publication and stale repair

- Commit `46efc7e7` repairs the priority Remesh prefix after the no-mesh distance sort and avoids re-sorting it when the aged ordering is already valid. Release `Cubatarium` built and the static executable verifier passed.
- The visible no-teleport route at speed scale `1` completed normally (`process_rc=0`, no teleport), focus `7→−20` (`27` chunks). Product quality remained red at `17/39` gates: holes `.9091`, Dirty median/max `268.5/429`, fly wall median `42.00 ms`, `chunk_not_ready` median `68.5`, and post-stop convergence=false. This single sequential run is not a controlled comparison with M289/M290.
- Focus-band census medians were `365` solid slices, `188` without drawable geometry, `32` accepted-empty, `127` with pending work, and `34` unresolved without work. The census band also includes water/low-world slices below the camera band, so those counts do not by themselves measure pixels visible from the route.
- The sampled pixel trace had `10` opaque voxel-ray hits with no corresponding opaque depth in `1,920` probes. Nine hits were in six new FirstMesh slices at mesh/publication revisions `1/0`, queue age `8–35`; the matching lifecycle records show those first publications completing `10–45 ms` after attempt creation. This is a short-lived load gap, not evidence of a multi-second mesh stall.
- The remaining sample hit `(-20,2,6)` at focus distance `3`, with a live packed draw path, mesh target/publication `2/1`, priority Remesh age `93`, index `34`. The watched trace accepted it at index `0`, age `126`, 33 epochs later, but no later publication stage for that attempt was retained. Its ray entered through `+Y` while the recorded face-debt bit was `−X`; the sample does not prove that an unknown-neighbor face caused the miss.
- M291 confirms queue-prefix repair is necessary but does not materially improve route-level rendering quality. Continue by joining a depth miss to the exact entry-face neighbor state and publication lifecycle; do not use the broad `unfinished_visual` rate as the sole visual correctness result.
- Artifacts: [M291 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m291_remesh_prefix_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and watched traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-010916_29048.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-010912.29048).

## M292: extra service for a sustained aged priority-remesh backlog

- The exact M291 gap hit gravel (`378`, opaque cube) after water (`575`, transparent fluid), entering the block from `+Y`. The current mesh had published geometry revision `1` while demand was at `2`; its live packed draw path had only nine quads and the priority remesh was at index `34/207`, age `93`. At frame epoch `2013` it was accepted at queue head, age `126`; the retained lifecycle trace records worker build and GPU queueing, but not a later publish for this attempt. This rules out the recorded `−X` face-debt as the ray-entry cause and points to stale geometry awaiting service.
- M292 keeps the shared per-frame scheduler cap and FirstMesh capture reservation, but lets the miss-path remesh lane use one additional capture/slot when at least four priority remeshes are at least 30 frames old inside the focus ring. The condition is disabled when admission intentionally steals remesh work for FirstMesh or when the capture pool cannot preserve the FirstMesh reserve. Release build and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
- The visible `product-174657-far` no-teleport run used speed scale `1`, reached focus `7→−20` (`27` chunks) at `5.70007` blocks/s, and the process exited normally. Harness product gates remained `17/39`, stop convergence failed, and the result is not a controlled A/B on a reused world. Directionally, direct opaque pixel-ray gaps fell `10→1`, holes rate `.9091→.8864`, priority-remesh schedules accepted `170→259`, median accepted age `127→113`, and maximum accepted queue index `109→93`. Focus-band no-drawable median moved `188→184` and unresolved-without-owner median `31→27.5`; Dirty median moved `268.5→284.5` while max moved `429→390`. FirstMesh schedules accepted fell `187→166`, so the added service shifted work between lanes rather than proving a net quality fix.
- The one M292 pixel gap was a new FirstMesh slice `(-22,2,4)`, queue index `66`, age `2`, with geometry `1/0` (target/publication). Its lifecycle trace shows publication about `10 ms` after attempt creation. That is consistent with a brief load gap; it does not explain the high unfinished-visual proxy or prove how the user's larger empty-looking regions render. The product stop-line and unresolved no-work counts remain open.
- Practice cross-check: Luanti coalesces mesh-update requests by mesh position, refreshes queued voxel input when that position is already pending, computes meshes on worker threads, and returns urgent results on a separate queue. This supports the audit's direction toward coalesced per-slice demand, immutable/refreshable inputs, and measured worker-versus-publication stages; it does not justify copying its mapblock scheduler as-is. [Luanti mesh update worker and queues](https://github.com/luanti-org/luanti/blob/master/src/client/mesh_generator_thread.cpp).
- Next investigate why the FirstMesh/Remesh lane caps and GPU-output readiness leave a large focus census unfinished even while the per-period scheduler reports positive service. Preserve the direct pixel witness, correlate queue admission through final publication for repeated coordinates, and reject this lane change if a controlled rerun shows the FirstMesh tradeoff outweighs the stale-remesh improvement.
- Artifacts: [M292 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m292_aged_remesh_extra_slot_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and watched traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-014549_18100.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-014545.18100).

## M293–M295: GPU queue age is not the focus FirstMesh bottleneck

- M293/M294 instrumented `ProcessPendingGpuMeshes` around actual readback-slot availability and pending phases. M293's profile path was relative to the executable working directory and produced no profile file; its visible no-teleport flight still ran at scale `1`, reached focus `7→−21`, and exited normally, with product gates red (`16/39`, holes `.795`, unfinished visual `70`). M294 corrected the path and recorded `795` samples: pending GPU work median `9`/p95 `13`, queued median `7`, and a free readback slot at each sampled call. The run reached `7→−20` at the expected `5.70007` blocks/s; product gates remained red (`18/39`, holes `.909`, unfinished `65`).
- M295 added queue age and kick-stop reasons. Its diagnostic instrumentation materially lowered throughput (median wall `86.1 ms`, `11.6` effective FPS, only `18` chunks covered), so it is not a performance comparison. Of `419` GPU-process samples, `415` began with a free readback slot. Focus-missing FirstMesh entries waiting in the GPU queue had median age `0.17 ms`, p95 `236 ms`, maximum `864 ms`; none reached one second. This does not support readback-fence latency as the cause of the long focus holes. The aggregate `gpu_not_ready` label therefore hides the relevant upstream wait. In contrast, some queued remesh work aged to nearly the full flight duration (`~80 s`), so stale drawable repair remains a separate starvation risk.
- A route-local census at `(-11,0,3)` found a solid 4,096-block slice at world minimum Y, target/published geometry `53/53`, desired/published coverage `1/0`, face and overlay debt only on `−Y`, and no Dirty queue entry. The `−Y` neighbor is outside the generated world (`y=-1`); all other peers are resident and solid. The slice repeatedly re-entered mesh work while the bottom-face debt could never be closed. This is evidence of a permanent boundary-debt loop and wasted FirstMesh service, not proof of a visible pixel gap: camera Y was `59`, and pixel probes in the same column at epochs `780/840` hit resident `y=2` terrain with no voxel/depth gap. A different view or world-bound policy could still make such a boundary visible.
- The bounded repair is to represent the known lower world boundary as known exterior during mesh capture, so the mesher can publish a stable bottom boundary instead of treating it as an unloaded neighbor. Keep ordinary missing side/bottom neighbors conservative, preserve seam remesh on real neighbor arrival, and verify that `y=0` debt settles without regressing visible gaps or adding a distant closing wall. Separately, the aged-remesh lane needs a later controlled flight without profiling overhead.
- Artifacts: [M293 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m293_gpu_process_profile_product174657_20261002.json), [M293 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-093554_38168.jsonl), [M294 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m294_gpu_process_profile_product174657_20261002.json), [M294 GPU profile](../../bin/logs/gpu_mesh_process_profile_m294.jsonl), [M295 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m295_gpu_phase_age_product174657_20261002.json), [M295 perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-101154_22780.jsonl), [M295 GPU profile](../../bin/logs/gpu_mesh_process_profile_m295.jsonl).

## M296: the world-floor debt closes; pixel gaps still follow stale drawable geometry

- Release build and static executable verification passed after classifying the absent neighbor below chunk `y=0` as known exterior in `ChunkMeshSnapshot::Capture`. The visible no-teleport flight used `World_164`, scale `1`, measured `5.70007` blocks/s, and covered focus `7→−21` (`28` chunks). The app exited normally (`process_rc=0`); product gates stayed red (`16/39`, stop gates `7/12`).
- All `62` retained focus-slice samples at chunk `y=0` reported no `−Y` face/overlay debt. This verifies the permanent lower-bound classification in the captured state; many such slices still had no mesh revision or queue owner, so FirstMesh admission remains a separate unresolved path.
- There were `7` voxel-hit/opaque-depth gaps in `1,840` pixel probes. One exact miss at `(-263,47,102)` hit opaque gravel in `(-17,2,6)`: the slice had 4,096 non-air voxels, target/published geometry `2/1`, a live packed mesh with 11 quads and six ready draw ranges, and a priority Remesh at queue head aged `66` frames. The pixel had no opaque depth hit. Two epochs later the watched trace accepted that revision from the same queue head. This is direct evidence that a live drawable can be present while its older geometry lacks the voxel face hit by the camera; missing texture, draw-gate denial, and absent GPU residency do not explain this sample.
- The lower-bound fix therefore closes a real non-terminating coverage debt but does not solve the renderer’s visible holes. The strongest current pixel evidence points to stale drawable coverage during content/revision changes and service latency in the Remesh lane. Next compare the exact ray-hit surface against source mesh faces and the submitted packed ranges, then trace why near-camera stale geometry remains visible while its successor is queued. Keep the last published image alive; if the missing face belongs to new content, consider a bounded provisional patch for that delta rather than widening Unknown-neighbor visibility globally.
- Artifacts: [M296 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m296_world_bottom_boundary_product174657_20261002.json), [M296 perf and pixel/focus traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-102829_3728.jsonl), [M296 INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-102822.3728).
## M297: visible repair priority survives requeue; unowned stale geometry remains

- M296's final miss at `(-21,3,3)` advanced a geometry target while the published mesh was still drawable. `EnsureVisualRepairDirtyPriority` promoted an already-existing target, but its new-target branch only called `MarkDirtyPriority`; for a drawable chunk that method intentionally creates an ordinary Remesh entry. When a live build owned the coordinate, promotion also had no persistent state to carry through `RemeshAfterApply`.
- The repair now promotes drawable work after both new-target and existing-target admission. `UChunkDirtySet` retains a detached priority request only while `RemeshAfterApply` explicitly owns the follow-up, transfers it to the front of `RemeshQ` when that ticket is enqueued, and clears it when the owner is erased or converted to FirstMesh. The Release `Cubatarium` target built successfully and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
- A visible no-teleport `product-174657-far` flight used scale `1`, reached focus `7→−21` (`28` chunks), and measured `5.70007` blocks/s. The application exited normally, while the product report remained red: holes rate `.8636`, unfinished-visual median `19`, focus miss stuck for `32 s`, `miss_end=1`, and `5/12` stop gates failed. Direct renderer probes found `10` voxel-hit/opaque-depth gaps among `1,760` samples. `21/39` flight gates failed overall. These values are not a controlled comparison with M296 because the same persistent world continued changing.
- The queue trace does confirm the local ordering repair: at `(-21,3,3)`, target/published geometry was `11/10`; its watched schedule later observed priority Remesh at index `0/84`, age `57` frames. The revision bump record itself is emitted before the post-admission promotion and therefore still reports ordinary kind `3`; the later schedule trace reports priority kind `2`. This demonstrates lane placement, not completion or timely visible recovery.
- The most actionable new pixel witness is at world `(-323,48,101)`, opaque gravel (`378`), in chunk `(-21,3,6)`. The probe had no opaque surface/depth hit; chunk mesh target/published geometry was `9/2`, with no dirty queue entry, no work-owner flags, and no active demand attempt. This is a much larger revision gap than M296's witness and points to an orphaned/unowned target rather than merely a low-priority queue. Another near-camera gap at `(-2,3,3)` did have a priority Remesh owner at index `67`, age `47`, so both missing ownership and slow owned service are present.
- Next trace every state transition that can advance `MeshRevisions.Current` beyond the published revision, and enumerate all cleanup, cancellation, unload, and stale-result branches that erase the last executable owner. At each terminal transition require either a matching publication or a durable Dirty/RAA/in-flight owner, while preserving the last drawable image. Do not treat lower `unfinished_visual` or queue kind `2` alone as visual recovery.
- Artifacts: [M297 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m297_persist_visible_remesh_priority_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and pixel/lifecycle traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-105615_35484.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-105610.35484).

## M298: an admission denial starts the unowned-geometry cooldown

- M298 repeated the visible no-teleport `product-174657-far` route with the existing `CUBATARIUM_RELIGHT_AUDIT` instrumentation enabled. It ran at the normal requested scale `1`, measured `5.99991` blocks/s, and covered focus `7→−21`; the app exited normally. The product report remained red: holes `.8889`, focus miss stuck `28 s`, `miss_end=1`, fly wall median `37.26 ms`, and stop convergence false. This is a diagnostic run on a reused world, not a controlled comparison with M297.
- The INFO log contains `100` unowned-geometry retry records: `95` ended with a concrete owner and `5` ended with `owner_after=0`. All five ownerless outcomes were `action=dirty_admit_denied`; for example chunk `(2,3,8)` had target/published geometry `3/2`, a drawable predecessor, settled light, and no Dirty, worker, RAA, GPU, capture, relight, or flow owner. Its cooldown was nevertheless updated by the common retry-note lambda. Repeated records for this coordinate were spaced about `2.4–3.0 s`, consistent with the configured two-second cooldown plus scan cadence.
- The retry lambda's comment says a census hit is not admission and cooldown begins only after an actual work/admission path, but its implementation stamps `last_unowned_geometry_retry_ms` even for the explicit `dirty_admit_denied` path. This creates a period where the demand has no executable owner and the retry scan deliberately suppresses its next attempt. Preserve the existing audit output, but gate the cooldown stamp on a concrete owner after the action and record the geometry revision actually admitted.
- The same run's pixel witness `(-323,48,101)` in chunk `(-21,3,6)` was a voxel hit without opaque depth at the capture point; that slice later acquired a priority Remesh owner at queue head. This reinforces that the gap is temporal and that the retry audit is needed to distinguish a brief census/admission window from a durable ownership leak.
- Artifacts: [M298 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m298_unowned_retry_owner_audit_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and focus/pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-110835_34108.jsonl), [INFO owner audit](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-110830.34108).

## M299: removing denial backoff causes per-frame retry churn

- M299 verified the M298 owner gate: a denied attempt no longer starts the long owner-backed cooldown. With `CUBATARIUM_RELIGHT_AUDIT` enabled, the flight recorded `363` unowned-geometry retry actions, including `102` `dirty_admit_denied` rows with `owner_after=0`. Repeated attempts for the same denied slice arrived every `30–100 ms`, rather than being hidden for roughly two seconds.
- The application exited normally and reached the west corridor at scale `1` (`5.70007` blocks/s, focus `7→−20`). The product result still failed: holes `.7955`, focus-miss stuck `30 s`, `miss_end=1`, visible-black focus median `10.5`, `23/39` flight gates failed, and stop convergence failed. This diagnostic run is not a controlled visual/performance comparison; detailed per-attempt INFO logging also makes its `46.62 ms` fly wall median unsuitable for performance conclusions.
- The correction must avoid both extremes: a denied budget admission should retry soon enough to avoid a multi-second unowned visual debt, while a per-frame retry loop produces redundant scans and log churn. Use a short bounded backoff for an unowned/denied result and retain the longer cooldown only after a concrete queue/worker/relight owner was admitted; keep the retry keyed to the post-action geometry revision.
- Artifacts: [M299 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m299_retry_cooldown_after_owner_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and focus/pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-111436_30220.jsonl), [INFO retry audit](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-111432.30220).

## M300: short backoff bounds denied geometry retries

- M300 introduced a per-record retry delay selected from the post-action owner state: `2,000 ms` after an executable owner is admitted and `250 ms` after an ownerless/denied attempt. The cooldown key is the geometry revision read after admission; the RelightAudit line records both owner state and selected delay. The Release `Cubatarium` target built and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
- A visible no-teleport flight used scale `1`, measured `5.70007` blocks/s, and reached focus `7→−21`. In the retry audit, `216` actions ended with an owner and the `2,000 ms` delay; `188` ended without an owner and used `250 ms`. Across repeated denied attempts, the observed gap had median `273 ms`, minimum `251 ms`, and p90 `660 ms`, compared with M299's `30–100 ms` frame-by-frame retry pattern. This confirms the short bounded throttle is active.
- Product quality remains unresolved: holes `.9091`, unfinished-visual median `25`, focus miss stuck `26 s`, `miss_end=1`, visible-black focus median `11`, and `24/39` flight gates failed; post-stop convergence remained false. The app exited normally and the speed stayed within the expected range. M298–M300 use a continuously changing `World_164` and M299/M300 enable verbose audit logging, so their wall times and gate deltas are diagnostic, not controlled A/B results.
- Next separate budget-denied candidates from queue-owned stale geometry and FirstMesh holes. The retry backoff is bounded, but the pixel/stop criteria still fail; trace what makes the long-lived focus miss at `cx=-21, cz=3` remain unsatisfied through stop, and test any change against the same route criteria without verbose profile overhead.
- Artifacts: [M300 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m300_short_denied_retry_backoff_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and focus/pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-112546_14780.jsonl), [INFO retry audit](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-112542.14780).

## M301: pixel witnesses separate unloaded rays from stale drawable holes

- Added the first-unloaded voxel coordinate and distance to the bounded renderer-pixel witness. This distinguishes a ray that exits known world data from a ray that actually reaches an opaque voxel behind missing rendered depth. The Release `Cubatarium` target built and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
- A visible no-teleport `product-174657` flight used movement scale `1`; median speed was `5.09994` blocks/s and focus moved `7→−11` (`18` chunks). The app stayed responsive and exited normally. The product report remained red: holes `.7674`, visible-black fly median/max `9/19`, `24/39` gates passed, stop convergence=false, and 14 spikes with a maximum of `347.46 ms`. This is a diagnostic run against the evolving `World_164`, not a controlled comparison.
- Across `2,048` renderer-pixel probes, the voxel ray ended at unloaded data in `1,139` samples, hit a loaded opaque voxel in `564`, and found no opaque voxel within its horizon in `345`. Only two samples were actual ray/depth gaps; therefore an unloaded-ray state alone is not evidence of a visually empty chunk.
- Both true gaps hit solid voxels in chunk `(-2,3,4)` at frame epoch `1320`, camera `(-15,58,56)`. The first hit `(-26,49,67)` at `18.002` blocks with no opaque depth surface; the second hit `(-26,49,69)` at `19.311` blocks while the recorded opaque surface was `23.854` blocks away. At capture the chunk was drawable, had three visible MDI commands, face-debt mask `63`, mesh/current geometry `6`, published geometry `5`, and an ordinary Remesh owner at index `77` with age `71` frames. This is direct evidence of a stale drawable mesh exposing a hole; it does not establish that all other visually empty slices contain drawable data.
- The screen-ray repair handler selects bounded ray-hit candidates and submits a ColumnFlow FirstMesh ticket, but it does not explicitly move an existing drawable geometry-debt owner into the priority Remesh lane. The candidate trace did not retain this exact pixel-hit slice during the run, so its coarse ray sampling is not yet a complete backstop for the pixel witness. Next promote only a screen-ray-selected drawable slice with current geometry debt, preserving its existing revision and owner; record whether its queue lane changes, then verify the same route's pixel gaps and repair latency.
- Artifacts: [M301 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m301_unloaded_voxel_witness_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-120020_34652.jsonl), [INFO log](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-120015.34652).

## M303: readiness cache now observes light-debt transitions

- Added incremental unfinished-column invalidation when column emerge state changes and when pending-light or sticky-light debt is inserted/cleared. This covers readiness transitions even when FIFO admission fails and no mesh Dirty callback fires. The Release target built and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
- The focus-slice trace identified a cache/admission mismatch: in M302, `319/508` unresolved/no-drawable samples had current pending-light/defer flags but cached visual class `Ready` and were absent from `unfinished_keys`. In M303, this combination fell to `70/516`; `446/516` unresolved samples were classified `MissingMesh` and present in the unfinished set. The focus-band census also moved from M302 medians `108` no-drawable / `43` unresolved-without-work to `87` / `30` in M303. The reused world and changed admission make these route metrics diagnostic rather than a controlled A/B, but the per-slice classification shift directly confirms the cache invalidation path is active.
- The visible route ran at `5.400235` blocks/s, west-focus delta `19`; process exit was `0` and analyzer product gates remained red. Holes rate was `.7674`, fly wall median `34.75 ms`, visible-black max `21`, and post-stop convergence=false. There were five pixel/depth gaps, all in chunk `(-2,3,4)` at frame epoch `1320`; mesh/current geometry was `5`, published geometry `4`, and its priority Remesh owner was at index `51`, age `2`. The screen-ray repair trace had promoted this same coordinate at that frame to kind `2`, index `0/121`, age `1` before later same-frame queue insertions. This exposes promotion churn as a separate issue: newer visible repairs can push a just-promoted stale slice behind dozens of peers before the renderer samples the frame.
- Preserve the cache invalidation. Next make promotion stable within the priority Remesh lane: repeated requests for an already-priority entry must not move it, and a new priority entry should not leapfrog older visible debt. Re-run the same route and compare exact pixel gaps, priority queue order, and eventual publication.

The slice had current/published geometry `18/17`, face-debt mask `63`, pending light, and a Remesh owner at ordinary queue index `150/155`; it also had a PromoteRelight column-flow ticket. The ray trace proves a real short-range stale-geometry hole, not an unloaded horizon.
- Screen-ray promotions were observed on this coordinate at frames `1140` and `1155`, first at priority Remesh `0/130` for geometry `10/9`, then `4/126` for `15/14`. Successor tickets were ordinary Remesh at indices `114–148`; geometry revision `18` published about `17.67 s` after its bump. This shows that the screen-ray prefix works for the ticket it promotes, but service/removal clears that marker before later face-debt successors are enqueued. The exact later gap therefore reappeared deep in the ordinary queue despite earlier repair of the same slice.

The retry throttle is active, but its admitted budget and the owners represented by flow/deferred-relight tickets remain separate concerns.
- Next carry screen-ray urgency through an active mesh/capture/GPU owner so a successor Dirty ticket inherits it after the current ticket is consumed. Keep only one bounded screen-ray marker per coordinate and clear it when the geometry target is published or the coordinate is retired. Verify the same slice's queue position across a screen-ray promotion, dequeue, `FaceDebtCallback`, successor enqueue, and publication; continue to report the global product gates separately.

The new reason identifies this path as an important source for the target slice, but the trace still lacks the actual expected/observed IDs and stamps needed to tell a legitimate input transition from a stale/mismatched upload. ## M309: material stamps reject the same IDs when batch order changes

- Added a bounded INFO trace for material-retain events on pixel-ray-pinned chunks. It records the expected and uploaded block-ID sequences, prior-to-new flips, material stamps, and chunk/pass revision context. The visible no-teleport flight exited normally at `5.1` blocks/s and west-focus delta `18`; product gates were `24/39`, holes `.814`, and fly wall median `47.34 ms`.
- The M309 trace captured 89 ray-pinned material retains. For `(-2,3,4)`, eight bounded examples showed expected IDs `[377,573,549,572]` and uploaded IDs `[377,549,572,573]`: the same unique ID set in a different order. The stamp is order-sensitive, so its expected value `11569350184576895676` differed from uploaded `15515291808441894594`; batch index `0` changed from prior block `572` to candidate `377`. The candidate sequence contained the CPU-expected `377` for that batch, so the rejection was caused by comparing lists in different orders rather than a bad material at that index. The logged `source_mesh_rev` field was the render-pass revision, not the target chunk's geometry revision; M310 renames it to avoid conflating those domains.
- M309 had 2,048 pixel probes and two exact voxel/depth gaps in this same chunk at epoch `960`; both sampled current/published geometry `4/3`, face-debt mask `63`, and priority Remesh index `11`. Its transition ring contained 34 `face_debt_material_retry` bumps for that coordinate. Across captured coordinates, material-retry bumps totaled `843`, while peer `face_debt_callback` bumps totaled `57`. This identifies a broad repeated Retain source, but the route-level holes remain a separate measure.
- Normalize expected and uploaded material IDs by stable `batchIndex`, and exclude uploaded entries that are not part of the current pass before computing the pass stamp. Keep the wrong-material rejection for a real block-ID mismatch at the same index.
- Artifacts: [M309 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m309_screen_ray_material_stamps_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-143554_36084.jsonl), [INFO material-retain trace](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-143550.36084).

The bounded pixel-ray material trace remains opt-in and capped. Release build and static executable verification passed; no tests were run.
