The packed opaque path was selected and ready, with a resident slot, 22 quads in 11 opaque ranges, 11 draw calls, and all 11 ranges texture-ready; CPU source indices were zero because this slice used the packed path. This is a post-publication packed-render gap, not a stale Dirty revision.
- The stamp fix removes a real retry loop, but does not resolve the remaining renderer issue. Next compare the exact packed ray hit with the packed-slot geometry and cull/depth path; do not classify the remaining gap as missing chunk data based on the unfinished-visual proxy.
- Artifacts: [M310 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m310_material_stamp_by_batch_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-144418_10160.jsonl), [INFO trace](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-144414.10160).

## M311: packed shader uses a different voxel-space origin than the CPU/MDI mesh

- Ran the same visible no-teleport product route with `render.gpu_packed_meshing=false`, restoring `bin/config.json` byte-for-byte afterward (SHA-256 `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C`). The app exited normally at `5.99991` blocks/s and west-focus delta `15`. This mode produced 1,600 pixel probes with zero voxel/depth gaps; the overall route remained red (`21/39` gates, holes `.864`, fly wall median `136.09 ms`).
- This is directional evidence only, not an exact A/B: the slower route sampled different camera positions. M310's gap was screen pixel `(864,90)` from camera `(93,63,56)`, hitting sand `(73,47,43)` at `28.9071` blocks with no opaque depth. M311 sampled the same screen pixel at camera X `114` and `102`, but not at the M310 camera pose; those rays had no gap. Its six samples touching voxel chunk `(4,2,2)` also had no gap, with CPU/MDI source and visible index counts `132`.
- Source comparison establishes a concrete geometry-contract mismatch: `GreedyMeshEmitter` and GPU vertex emission anchor local faces at `slice/u/v ± 0.5`, while packed quads stored integer `slice/u/v` and the pulling shader used those integers directly before applying face axes. Thus every packed face was displaced by half a block relative to the CPU/MDI mesh and voxel grid. Updated both the active packed vertex shader and its embedded reference GLSL to subtract `0.5` from the decoded local origin before face-specific offsets. Recheck packed-on pixel-ray gaps in Release; keep the M311 route-wide holes and frame cost separate from this geometry-path diagnosis.
- Artifacts: [M311 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m311_packed_meshing_off_product174657_20261002.json), [perf and pixel traces](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-145505_33712.jsonl), [INFO trace](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-145500.33712).

## M312: packed-origin fix passes the target chunk probes; one independent MDI debt gap remains

- Built Release and ran the visible, no-teleport route with `gpu_packed_meshing=true`. The process exited `0`, movement-speed median was `5.99991` blocks/s, and west-focus delta was `31`. The report remained red (`15/39` gates, unfinished-visual holes `.817`, fly wall median `48.46 ms`, visible-black focus median `12`, stop convergence=false).
- M312 captured 2,048 renderer pixel probes. It recorded no voxel/depth gap in `(4,2,2)`; packed drawing was selected there with 22 quads, 11/11 texture-ready ranges, and 11 draw calls. The exact M310 camera pose `(93,63,56)` was not sampled in M312 (its nearby samples were at X `104` and `85`), so this is not a strict same-ray A/B. It does confirm that the packed chunk/range path remains drawable after the coordinate correction.
- The only M312 pixel-ray gap was in a different MDI chunk `(-2,3,3)`: grass block `377` at `(-17,48,61)`, camera `(37,62,56)`, screen pixel `(608,270)`. The visible MDI source had 36 indices, but chunk geometry was current/published `2/1`, face-debt mask `29`, and an owned Remesh sat at dirty queue index `63` with age `48` frames; relight was also in flight. No opaque depth was recorded. This points to an in-flight stale/partial MDI mesh rather than the packed vertex origin.
- The packed coordinate fix is supported by the source-level mesh contract and target-chunk observations, but it does not fix route-wide incomplete visuals. Continue with the MDI stale/partial publication and streaming repair owner; do not fold this separate gap into the packed-path result.

## M313: a column Flow ticket can mask slice-level geometry debt

- Commit `6a131d7b` removes the `!flow_after` gate from direct screen-ray slice admission. `HasRepairTicket` answers whether the horizontal column is in ColumnFlow; it does not prove that the exact Y slice has a live Dirty/build/GPU owner. The existing direct-mark helper checks the slice's drawable/debt state and its concrete mesh owners before creating work. When it marks a slice, its trace now takes precedence over the column-ticket label.
- Release build and static executable verification passed. The visible `product-174657-far` no-teleport run exited normally (`process_rc=0`, not timeout-killed), moved at `6.00002` blocks/s, and covered focus `7→−27` (`34` chunks). Route gates remained red (`19/39`): unfinished holes `.716`, fly wall median `78.23 ms`, stop convergence=false.
- In the target chunk `(-2,3,3)`, frame `795` logged `first_mesh_slice_dirty_direct` while a FirstMesh ticket still existed. Its queue was empty just before the mark; the exact slice reported `mesh_scheduled_this_frame=1`. At frame `810`, the same chunk had a new geometry successor (`10/9`) at priority Remesh index `1`. This confirms that the screen-ray witness can recover slice ownership even when a column ticket is already present; it does not establish route-wide visual recovery.
- The sole voxel/depth gap in `1,680` renderer probes was a different first-publication slice: sand block `549` at `(-143,40,161)`, chunk `(-9,2,10)`, distance `123.275`. The resident chunk had `4,076` non-air blocks but no mesh revision/publication (`0/0`), no mesh owner, and pending light. The screen-ray path promoted its relight FIFO entry from index `9/15` to `0/15`; about `14` seconds later the relight handoff queued FirstMesh work. In the same frame, another selected stale drawable at `(-9,2,7)` received direct slice repair. Thus the exact new-geometry ray hit still waited behind the primary stale-geometry witness.

## M315: relight throughput must use final Apply installs, not ring occupancy

- The analyzer used `relight_completed_n` (completion-ring occupancy after Apply) for `cruise_relight_completed_med` and for a first-to-last “throughput” delta. The engine drains that ring in the same frame; `RelightFifoPolicy.h` explicitly warns not to interpret its occupancy as throughput. The period counter `relight_apply_final_n` increments when a relight result finishes its final Apply/install, so the analyzer now uses that counter for completion median, spike median, and per-moving-period mean. The ring value remains available as the explicitly named `cruise_relight_completion_ring_occupancy_med`. The existing product phase gate already uses `cruise_relight_apply_final_med` and was not changed.
- Reanalysis of raw period rows corrects M313 from `0` to a final-Apply median of `2` per moving period (87 installs over 60 moving periods; mean `1.475`). M314 corrects to median `1` (86 installs over 60 periods; mean `1.458`). The completion-ring occupancy median is still `0` in both. Thus the zero legacy value did not mean relight made no progress; assess whether that progress reaches the screen-ray slice and hands off a useful mesh separately.
- M313/M314 route gates and original product reports were not regenerated by a new flight. The corrected analyzer ran over the saved raw perf logs and still returned red overall analysis; its updated relight values do not change the already-red route conclusion. This corrects the earlier interpretation that relight throughput was absent and avoids using ring occupancy to justify more capture budget.
- Artifacts: [M313 corrected analysis](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m313_reanalysis_metrics_20261002.json), [M314 corrected analysis](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m314_reanalysis_metrics_20261002.json), [M313 raw perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-153318_17668.jsonl), [M314 raw perf](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-154855_30544.jsonl).

The corresponding ray hits were real sand blocks beneath the transparent pass; separate CPU census confirmed visible water chunks with `256` non-air blocks and fluid top faces. This is not an empty chunk or missing terrain payload: the water draw darkens pixels over resident geometry.

## M319: dense pixels separate water-light previews from missing opaque geometry

- The visible no-teleport Release flight exited normally at `6.000` blocks/s and moved focus `7→−32` (`624` blocks). This was a west-route run, not the `8,192`-block far-distance checkpoint. Product gates remained red (`15/39` passed): holes `.8667`, fly wall median `68.05 ms`, deferred-far relight peak `509`, and post-stop convergence=false. The application completed; the harness returned quality-gate failure.
- Dense tracing recorded `2,048` renderer pixels. Ten direct opaque-ray gaps were found, all over resident `4,096`-non-air-block chunks. Nine gaps were on GL scanline `y=315`, equivalent to top-left normalized Y `.5625`; the remaining gap was at GL `y=135`, top-left `.8125`. The production screen-ray grid still samples only `.125/.375/.625/.875`, so it does not test either exact dense-probe row. At epoch `2040`, matching X `480`, the production `.625` ray hit chunk `(-32,2,4)` at `42.75` blocks, while the pixel's `.5625` ray hit unpublished chunk `(-37,2,6)` at `120.23` blocks and found no opaque depth. This is direct evidence that the current vertical sampling can see a nearer, satisfied slice while missing the distant first-mesh slice behind it.
- Eight of the nine far-row misses had no published geometry (`published_geom_rev=0`); several also had no desired geometry revision or live mesh owner at the pixel sample. The remaining far-row miss had `desired/published=2/1` and a Dirty owner. These are separate lifecycle states, so row alignment can expose and admit demand but does not by itself solve queue latency or publication. One additional gap at epoch `1440`, pixel `(864,135)`, already had current geometry (`4/4`); the first CPU opaque voxel was `2.14` blocks closer than the rendered opaque depth, which came from adjacent chunk `(-17,2,2)`. Treat that sample as a distinct mesh coverage/seam investigation, not as a first-mesh wait.
- The ten dark-blue water samples are a different set of pixels from the ten direct gaps. They have a transparent fragment (`alpha=206`), an exact sea-plane water hit, and no voxel-ray gap. At epoch `1680`, adjacent samples on GL `y=315` change from normal water `(25–30,118–132,210–218)` to `(11,53,91–100)`. The darker pixels map to water slices with `published_light_rev=0`, no current light settlement, and gate bit `22` (`ShouldDrawProvisionalLightPreview`); the adjacent bright slices have published/settled light revision `1`. The greedy fragment shader's provisional floor is `lit=max(lit,0.42)`, which matches the dark-to-normal RGB ratio. Thus these bounded dark rectangles are rendered water under a deliberate ambient preview while lighting is unsettled, not empty water geometry. Keep that lighting presentation issue distinct from the opaque geometry holes; do not remove the preview while waiting on relight.
- Next, add a bounded screen-ray row at `.5625` and verify same-frame candidate selection and owner acquisition for exact pixel gaps. Preserve the existing four rows. The `.8125` current-geometry gap and the water preview contrast remain separate follow-ups; no conclusion here says the remaining broad unfinished-visual metric is solved.

The process exited normally (`process_rc=0`, outcome `success`); product gates remained red (`26/39`), holes `.9067`, fly wall median `55.65 ms`, visible-black maximum `21`, deferred-far relight peak `141`, and post-stop convergence=false.
- Across `2,048` dense pixel probes, `706` rays hit opaque voxels. All `706` exact entry faces were present in the current CPU opaque batch, linked to a pooled GPU command with a visible instance, and had a nonzero texture ID. The oracle found zero direct opaque-ray/depth gaps. At pixel `(864,135)`, 13 route samples had exact face matches and no depth gap. This does not replay M321's exact world coordinate or establish full-frame coverage: the world/route state changed, and the sampler checks only `20` X positions on `8` scanlines per capture.
- The route's aggregate `unfinished_visual` hole rate remained `.9067` while the sparse direct-pixel oracle found no depth gap. These measurements cover different things; the pixel result does not explain the user's empty-looking chunks, and the aggregate readiness proxy does not identify which viewport pixels are empty. In the same trace, `870/2,048` rays met unloaded chunk data first and `472` found no opaque voxel within range; those outcomes include top-of-screen sky rays and must not all be counted as terrain holes.
- Next, carry this exact-hit join into a true no-teleport run through the `8,192`-block checkpoint, with broader viewport coverage or captured frames at the periods where `unfinished_visual` remains nonzero. Join each apparent empty region to actual ray hit, unloaded boundary, current CPU face, and submitted draw command before changing meshing, material fallback, or global scheduler budgets.
- Artifacts: [M322 report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m322_exact_hit_face_source_product174657_20261002.json), [raw perf/pixel trace](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-200956_24316.jsonl), [INFO trace](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-200951.24316).

## M323: the true-far run stalls before its checkpoint under render/stream load

- A visible Release no-teleport `product-174657-far` run was configured for the `8,192`-block checkpoint at `5.99991` blocks/s. It was allowed to reach the harness's 2,400-second timeout; the runner then killed the unresponsive process (`hang_killed=true`, `process_rc=124`). This was not a completed route or a far-checkpoint result. The last complete telemetry reached focus `7→−168` (about `2,800` blocks / `175` chunks), with roughly `6,800` resident chunks. The partial JSONL ended mid-record, so route adequacy, west coverage, and stop-line analysis were correctly left `UNTESTED`/failed rather than inferred from the partial run.
- Before the UI stopped producing frames, 333 period records showed a `.9728` unfinished-visual rate, dirty backlog max `810`, deferred-far relight max `488`, frontier pressure red for the whole route, and about `10.9` effective FPS. The final captured period had `scene_ms=206 ms`; nearby spikes reached `1,527 ms`. Renderer sub-timers show several contributors rather than one proven root: opaque refresh reached `531.8 ms` (last valid sample `280.9 ms`), opaque cull reached `1,420 ms`, and transparent draw reached `1,063 ms`; on the `280.9 ms` refresh sample, opaque GPU draw was `18.4 ms`. These measurements localize the stall to frame/render work as well as the streaming backlog, but do not by themselves distinguish CPU table/publication cost, driver synchronization, or GPU back-pressure.
- The process had stopped emitting frame/perf records after focus `−168` and the GUI was unresponsive for the remainder of the harness timeout. The final JSONL line was truncated by process termination. Preserve the partial logs as diagnostic evidence; do not treat the analyzer's partial counters as route acceptance.
- This run used `CUBA_VISUAL_BLACK_TRACE=1`, whose sampled full-frame diagnostics perform additional pixel/depth and GPU residency inspection. The trace may amplify sampled frame cost; because the freeze occurred during an instrumented run, repeat the same Release route with visual-black tracing disabled before assigning the stall to production rendering. The visible blackout issue and the responsiveness failure remain separate acceptance failures.
- The experiment restored user settings byte-for-byte: `bin/config.json` SHA-256 `4470F266079D264E4E5BC8C5E484D4E44995E0E41D11851153DF138CD059367C`; `bin/worlds/World_164/users.json` SHA-256 `8DB42B771256BD27944553C803183E5A89B4F26CCB0211E8990122DDA5AC6B66`.
- Next: repeat no-teleport with the same Release executable, route, world, speed, and GUI but without pixel/visual-black probes. Capture per-stage frame progress and distinguish ordinary long frames from a single non-returning call. If it remains responsive, then attribute the instrumented probe overhead; if it stalls again, use a process dump/ETW stack at the last frame to identify the blocking or runaway call before changing renderer or streaming policy. Keep the visual readiness states explicit as `Unknown`, `Pending`, `VerifiedEmpty`, `Drawable/Published`, and `Submitted/Culled` so empty-looking terrain is not conflated with absent voxel data.
- Artifacts: [M323 partial report](../../bin/suite_reports/engine_refactor/m323_true_far_checkpoint_product174657_20261002.json), [partial perf JSONL](../../bin/logs/perf_20261002-202019_36208.jsonl), [INFO trace](../../bin/logs/Cubatarium.exe.TIMLENOVO.Bakhshiev.log.INFO.20261002-202015.36208).
